Traced through the history: ⭐ **Origin — `5cda84ab`, and it is operational, not preservative**: *Rig A (#1) is in emulation mode holding the NexWatch slot; **put it back to `hw mode -r` before using it as a reader***. The same bullet says *both Chameleons carry the current build*. It is a reminder that a device is sitting in emulation MODE, nothing more. ⭐ **Drift — C289 (`fc47a0ab`)** listed it as one of three reasons not to flash #1. ⛔ But C289's load-bearing argument was neither: it checked **by per-file diff** whether that day's firmware changes could reach the emulate arms, found they could not, and concluded re-running them would prove nothing. That was sound. The slot was an aside. ⛔⛔ **Propagation — mine.** The compressed form *#1 (deliberately un-reflashed, holds the NexWatch slot)* went into the loop prompt I wrote, which restated it at the top of roughly twenty consecutive ticks. Repetition is what turned a transient note into a standing constraint, and it is why the operator remembered it as something being held for a test. ⇒ **Nothing in the notes or history instructs holding the Flipper in any state.** The real constraint is narrower and still true: the Proxmark cannot hear PWM-on-the-coil, so the Flipper is the only reader that can grade an emulate arm. ⚠ **C289's diff argument is now thoroughly stale**: #1 was built at `68042db`, and **16 firmware files have changed since, 14 of them LF**. It is not a useful reference state either. ⚠ **One of C289's three reasons DOES still stand** — the `enter_dfu` targeting hazard: the flash script does not choose its target, so DFU must be triggered on the intended port and exactly one `nordicDfu` device confirmed before programming | 3 commits traced, 1 operator correction, 16 changed files counted | ⭐ the origin commit's own wording settles it — no inference required | L332 |
| C364 | ⭐⭐⭐ **#1 REFLASHED AND THE EMULATE COLUMN IS UNBLOCKED — but `emugrade.sh` does not yet produce a result, and three of its bugs were mine.** The operator confirmed the NexWatch-slot claim was a misunderstanding (C363) and authorised a flash. **#1 now runs `v2.2.0-670-g90be1e2`**, current firmware, with #2 untouched — so emulate grading needs **no bench change at all**: #1 already faces the Flipper. ⚠ **The `enter_dfu` hazard is real and cost two attempts**: nrfutil run immediately after the trigger finds nothing, because #1 has not finished enumerating as `nordicDfu`; the bootloader then times out. A 3-second settle between trigger and program fixes it. #2 was confirmed on its own port before and after. ⭐ **The emulator works**: #1 on slot 1 (EM410X `DEADBEEF88`) is read by the Flipper, so the device emulates and the Flipper hears it. ⛔⛔ **But Indala on scratch slot 8 reads 0 of 6, and three instrument bugs were found on the way without reaching the cause.** (1) The null check leaves the device in READER mode and nothing switched it back, so every arm was scored against a device emulating nothing — with a clean null either side that read like confirmation. (2) `econfig` writes the CREDENTIAL but not the slot's LF TYPE, so slot 8 stayed `(disabled)undef` and emitted nothing however correct the credential was. (3) **The firmware said so on the very first run** — `WARNING: Slot LF type is not Indala` — and my own error filter matched `error|usage|invalid|Traceback` and not `WARNING`, so the answer was printed and thrown away. All three are fixed and it is still 0 of 6. ⇒ **Recorded as unfinished rather than as a firmware result.** A battery that has never produced a positive number is not evidence about the emulate arms; the next step is to compare slot 8/Indala against slot 1/EM410X, which works, rather than to keep patching the script | 1 flash, 1 known-good arm confirmed, 3 script bugs fixed | ⭐ the EM410X arm is the positive control and it passes, which is what makes the Indala zero a real question rather than a dead bench | L333 |
| C365 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **THE EMULATE COLUMN OPENS: A SLOT NEEDS FOUR THINGS, NOT TWO — and the PSK1 family emulates 6 of 6 across the board.** C364 left `emugrade.sh` at 0 of 6 with three of my own bugs already fixed. The cause was a fourth thing nothing in these notes had ever written down. ⛔⛔ **A slot emits only when ALL FOUR are true**: the **TYPE** is set (`hw slot type -s N -t <Type>`), the **CREDENTIAL** is written (`lf <proto> econfig -s N`), the **LF INTERFACE IS ENABLED** (`hw slot enable -s N --lf`), and the device is in **EMULATOR MODE** (`hw mode -e`). Miss the enable and `hw slot list` prints **`LF: (disabled)EM410X`** — type present, interface off, nothing on the air. ⭐ The tell was in the listing the whole time: the working slot 1 reads `LF: Empty EM410X`, the dead slot 8 read `LF: (disabled)EM410X`. ⭐ **Isolated in one step by putting the KNOWN-GOOD protocol on the SUSPECT slot**: EM410X on slot 8 also failed, which ruled out Indala and PSK and pointed straight at the slot. Enabling the interface took it to 3 of 3 immediately. ⭐⭐ **With that, the PSK1 family emulates and the bracket is clean: Indala 6/6, IDTECK 6/6, Keri 6/6, NexWatch 6/6, every one with its ASK arm at 0 of 6** and the reader-mode null clean before and after. The wrong-modulation arm is the built-in control (C83) and it holds for all four. ⚠ **Four instrument bugs in one battery, all mine** — reader mode never restored, slot type never set, a `WARNING` my error filter did not match, and now the interface never enabled. ⇒ The firmware was right every time and said so in plain text twice | 4 PSK arms x 6 reads, plus a slot-swap control | ⭐ the control varies the SLOT while holding the protocol fixed, which is what separated a slot problem from a protocol one | L334 |
| C366 | ⛔⛔ **SUPERSEDED BY C367 — THE BOUNDARY I DREW DOES NOT EXIST.** This claimed the ASK/Manchester emulate arms specifically do not emit, and separated them from EM410X on the grounds that EM410X uses the GPIO/comparator emitter and read 3 of 3. **EM410X also reads 0 of 4 when re-tested**, with the same signature, so there is no ASK/Manchester-versus-EM410X split. The measurements stand — Gallagher, Securakey, Noralsy and HID Prox each 0 of 6, PSK1 6 of 6 — but the boundary drawn around them was wrong, and it was drawn from a single EM410X control taken earlier in the session under different state | see C367 | ⛔ retained as written so the error is visible | L335, L336 |
| C367 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **THE EMULATOR'S PWM IS NOT PLAYING OUR SEQUENCE — the device's own instrument says so, and it is not about which protocol.** `hw lfdebug` on every failing arm reports **`SEQ[0].PTR ours: False ⛔ PWM0 IS NOT READING OUR SEQUENCE`**, with `PWM0 ENABLE: 1` and **`base clock: 125kHz`** — enabled, correct rate for ASK, playing somebody else's buffer. Twice with a stale pointer `0x2000CC2C`, once with a **null** `0x00000000`. ⛔⛔ **Two hypotheses of mine died here, and the device killed both.** (1) *The ASK/Manchester emitter is broken* (C366) — **EM410X fails too**, and it uses a different emitter. (2) *The PSK1→ASK transition leaves the clock wrong* — `gallagher.c`'s own comment says ASK needs 125kHz, and **lfdebug reports 125kHz**. The clock is right; the pointer is wrong. ⭐ **What is solid**: EM410X emulated **3 of 3 earlier in this same session** and reads **0 of 4** now, same slot, same credential — so this is a STATE the device gets into, not a protocol that cannot work. Gallagher fails even from a clean reboot. PSK1 read 6 of 6 throughout. ⛔ **What is NOT established, and matters**: which action loads the sequence and which leaves it null; whether older firmware behaves differently; and therefore whether *regression* is the right word at all. ⚠ The signature matches C129 — *`lf_sense_disable()` nulls `m_pwm_seq` and `lf_sense_enable()` never reloads it* — which F10 claims to have fixed. That is a lead, **not a conclusion**: F10's own regression check is one of the two `fixcheck.sh` cannot run without rig A. ⇒ Stopped here deliberately rather than iterate further on a live device late in a long session | 3 lfdebug dumps, 1 clean reboot, 4 arms, 2 control protocols | ⭐ `hw lfdebug` exists precisely to say whether a value is wrong or the peripheral is ignoring a right one (C149), and it answered in one call what four rounds of black-box testing did not | L336 |
| C368 | ⭐⭐⭐ **THE EMULATE FAILURE SURVIVES A REBOOT WITH THE SLOT CORRECTLY CONFIGURED — `m_pwm_seq` IS NEVER SET, AND THREE WORKAROUNDS ARE RULED OUT.** Continuing U18 from C367. Gallagher on slot 8: config **persists across a reboot** (`hw slot list` reports `LF: Gallagher`, slot active, emulator mode) and `hw lfdebug` still reports **`SEQ[0].PTR ours: False 0x00000000`** — a NULL pointer, so the modulator never ran. ⛔ **Ruled out, each by trying it**: bouncing the active slot (`hw slot change` away and back) does not populate it; `hw slot store` then bouncing does not; **a full reboot with the credential persisted does not**. So it is not a stale-RAM or load-ordering problem that a reload fixes. ⭐ **The code shows the shape of the failure**: `lf_tag_data_loadcb_inner()` is a chain of branches each guarded by `buffer->length >= <size>`, and a buffer that is too short **silently skips the modulator and leaves `m_pwm_seq` NULL** — no error, no log, nothing the host can see. Gallagher's guard is `LF_GALLAGHER_TAG_ID_SIZE` = **12 bytes**, and `lf gallagher econfig --raw` writes exactly 24 hex digits = 12 bytes. ⇒ The guard SHOULD be satisfied, so either the buffer is not reaching the loader or the loader is not being called for this type. **Not resolved, and I am not guessing which.** ⚠ **A silent length guard is itself worth flagging**: whatever the cause, a loader that skips its work when the data is short and reports nothing is why four rounds of black-box testing found nothing. The host sees a correctly configured slot in emulator mode and an antenna that is silent. ⇒ **Stopped deliberately.** This is a firmware investigation that deserves a fresh session, not the tail of a very long one, and the next step is instrumentation inside `lf_tag_data_loadcb_inner` rather than more black-box probing | 1 reboot, 3 reload strategies, 2 code paths read | ⭐ each ruled-out workaround is a real result: the bug is not in the reload path | L337 |
| C369 | ⛔⛔ **I TOLD THE OPERATOR COMPACTING DOES NOT SAVE USAGE. IT DOES, AND THE ARITHMETIC IS NOT SUBTLE.** My claim was that compaction *costs a summarisation pass and then re-expands*, so it is worth doing to avoid losing context rather than to economise. Wrong. **Every turn re-sends the whole window**, so carrying a large context pays for it again on *every subsequent turn*: N further turns cost N x large. Compacting costs **one** pass at the large size and then N x small. For any N above about one, compaction is cheaper — and the summary does not re-expand, which was the whole error. The app's own prompt (*keeps later replies fast and lighter on your usage*) is correct and I contradicted it. ⭐ **It is now measurable rather than guessed.** `utility-scripts/claude/context_check.sh` reports the same breakdown the GUI popover shows, computed locally — **no model call, no tokens, no credential** — in ~1.7s. This session at the time of writing: **`pct=89`, 891,924 of 1,000,000, msgs=846,059, free=75,044**. ⇒ Wired into `./autopilot.sh status` alongside `usage_check.sh`, which had **moved** to the same folder — the old Momentum path had been silently failing, so the loop was pacing blindly and saying *usage unavailable* without anyone reading it. Thresholds: **≥80% says compact at the end of this tick**, ≥60% says consider it. ⚠ **Why this one matters more than a wrong technical claim usually would**: the operator asked a direct question about their own tooling, I answered confidently from reasoning rather than checking, and the answer argued against a habit that was saving them money. A cheap local script settled it in one call | 1 script run, 1 arithmetic correction | ⭐ the measurement is free and repeatable, which is why it belongs in `status` rather than in anyone's memory | L338 |
| C370 | ⛔⛔ **THE CONTEXT CHECK I SHIPPED ONE COMMIT AGO REPORTED 4% WHEN THE TRUE FIGURE WAS 90%.** I flagged the `-c` fallback as unverified in the same breath as shipping it, then tested it. `-c` means *the most recent session in `$PWD`*, and sessions are keyed by the directory they **started** in — the repo root, not `research/indala-psk-read` where the loop runs `status`. Run from there it finds nothing, **silently measures a FRESH session's fixed overhead**, and prints **`pct=4 msgs=0`**. The status line read *context 4% used* at a moment when the real answer was 90%. ⛔⛔ **A check that reports green when it should be red is worse than no check** — it would have sat there reassuring every tick while the window filled. Same failure shape as the HEAD-comparison drift check two ticks earlier (C358), which went red on its own commit; that one announced itself, this one would not have. ⇒ **Two fixes, because one is not enough.** (1) Run it with the repo root as cwd, so `-c` resolves to the real session. (2) **Treat `msgs=0` as *not our session* and refuse to report a number**, rather than passing on a plausible-looking 4%. Verified from the loop's own directory with no session id set: it now reads **90%**, and the guard rejects a synthetic `msgs=0` line while accepting a real one. ⚠ **The lesson is about the flag, not the arithmetic**: `-c` is a convenience whose meaning depends on cwd, and a script that runs from a subdirectory inherits that silently. The explicit session id has no such ambiguity and is preferred where it is available | 1 comparison across two directories, 2 guard arms tested | ⭐ the discriminator was free — running the same command from two cwds and noticing `msgs=0` | L339 |
| C371 | ⭐ **NOTHING CAN TYPE `/compact` FOR A SESSION — four channels checked in the 2.1.269 bundle, not assumed.** (1) Scheduled-task and `/loop` fires enqueue with `skipSlashCommands:!0` **hardcoded**, so a cron prompt whose text is `/compact` arrives as literal text; (2) peer messages — the `SendMessage` tool and the `deliver` verb on `/tmp/cc-socks/<pid>.sock` — set the same flag alongside `isMeta:!0`; (3) the control protocol has **no compact verb** (`apply_flag_settings` and `update_settings` exist but are host→agent over the SDK stream, unreachable from a shell); (4) `PreCompact`/`PostCompact` hooks only observe and **block** — the bundle's own string is `Compaction blocked by PreCompact hook`. ⭐ The only lever is the auto-compact **threshold**, `autoCompactWindow`: a settings key, range **100k–1M tokens**, superseded by `$CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`, and read from `user,project,local` — this app launches with `--setting-sources=user,project,local` and **no** `--autocompact` flag, so a project settings file is authoritative. ⛔ **What this entry originally claimed beyond that — that lowering it trips a compact on the next turn — is false and is retracted by C372** | 4 delivery channels read in the bundle | ⭐ `strings` on the binary settled in minutes what no amount of black-box poking would have | L340, L341 |
| C372 | ⛔⛔ **I SHIPPED A COMPACT TRIGGER THAT DOES NOT TRIGGER, AND SAID SO IN THE COMMIT MESSAGE BEFORE TESTING IT.** C371 found the only lever — `autoCompactWindow` — and I wrote `compact_request.sh` to drop it below the session's current size, claiming *the next turn trips auto-compact by itself*. It does not. **Two independent trials:** armed at 159,960 against a measured 199,950 and later at 167,147 against 208,934, each crossing turn boundaries with the file in place; neither compacted, and the first only ended because its own TTL expired and disarmed it. ⭐ **The discriminator was free and I should have run it first**: `context_check.sh` forks a FRESH CLI, and with the file present that fresh process reported **`window=167147`** while the running session kept behaving as 1M. So the setting is honoured — **at process start, and only there**. The bundle does contain a live path (`notifyAutocompactInputsChanged`, fired when `w.settings !== P.settings`), which is what I reasoned from; a settings-FILE write does not reach it. The desktop app's own picker changes it live because it sends `apply_flag_settings` down the control stream, and that is precisely the channel a shell script cannot reach. ⇒ **`compact_request.sh` is deleted rather than kept as a near-miss**, and replaced by `utility-scripts/claude/autocompact.sh`, which sets the threshold for a PROJECT and says plainly that it takes effect at session start. ⚠ **The lesson is the ordering, not the mechanism.** I had a free, local, seconds-long check that would have falsified the claim before it reached a commit message, and I ran it afterwards. Same shape as C370: reasoning from the source is not evidence about a running process | 2 arm trials across 3 turn boundaries, 1 fresh-process comparison | ⭐ a forked CLI reading the same file is the control that separates *not read* from *not read yet* | L341 |
| C373 | ⛔⛔⛔ **U18 IS RETRACTED IN FULL: THE EMULATOR DOES SET `m_pwm_seq`, AND EVERY EMULATE ARM WAS SCORED AGAINST A FLIPPER WHOSE `rfid` COMMAND WAS NOT RUNNING.** U18 rested on `hw lfdebug`'s `SEQ[0].PTR ours: False` and concluded the loader silently skipped the modulator on a short buffer. **`hw emudebug` answers the actual question and was never asked**: with Gallagher configured on slot 8 it reports **`have pwm seq : True`** and **`frames per burst : 21`** — the loader ran, took its branch, and `recompute_frames_per_burst()` walked a real sequence. What is zero is **`playbacks started`**, and playback is started from exactly one place, `lpcomp_event_handler(UP)`, which needs a reader's field. ⛔ **There was no field.** The Flipper answers `rfid read` and `rfid emulate` with one red line — `failed to load external command` — because `rfid` is a plugin (`/ext/apps/RFID/lfrfid.fap`) and the loader refuses a .fap built against a different API than the running firmware. SD is healthy (exFAT, 57 GiB free, `lfrfid.fap` 66304b present); the firmware is the operator's own fork, **`mntm-dev` API 87.47, branch `t5577-deep-read`, built 12-09-2026**. ⇒ `SEQ[0].PTR` NULL is the CORRECT reading for a tag that has never been asked to transmit, not a defect. ⭐ **The control that settled it was free and took one command**: Indala PSK1 — the arm §1 recorded as 6/6 — scores 0/4 in this same session with `playbacks 0`, so the failure is not protocol-specific and never was | 2 protocols x 4 Flipper attempts, 5 emudebug dumps, 1 verbose CLI trace, 3 Flipper storage/info probes | ⭐ `--verbose` on our own reader script, which prints the raw CLI lines the scoring threw away | L342 |
| C374 | ⛔⛔ **OUR OWN READER SCRIPT TURNED A DEAD INSTRUMENT INTO A CLEAN `0/N`, AND THAT IS WHAT MADE C373 SURVIVE A WHOLE UNIT.** `flipper.py` rejects a malformed command structurally — `Available protocols:`, a usage banner, `Unknown protocol` — and raises rather than scoring. **A refused plugin load matches none of them**: it is one red line followed by an ordinary prompt, so `decode()` found no success line, returned `None`, and the attempt scored `-`. Eleven emulate arms then reported `0/6` with a clean null either side, which reads as *the emulator is silent* when it means *the reader is not running*. ⛔ The script's own banner even says the right thing — *this is a null with no positive control (F05)* — and the run above printed it while I read the number instead. ⇒ `failed to load external command` is now in `REJECTED`, so the first attempt aborts; verified on the live bench, which now stops on attempt 1 rather than returning `0/3`. ⚠ **The general rule this earns**: a structural rejection list must be driven by *how the instrument fails*, not by *how a user mistypes* — those are different sets, and only the first protects a measurement | 1 fix, verified against the failing bench | ⭐ the failure is silent by construction, so only running the instrument against a KNOWN-good arm exposes it | L342 |
| C375 | ✅ **C129/C130 ARE GENUINELY FIXED, AND `emudebug`'s *changing a slot's LF tag type kills emulation until a power cycle* IS STALE TEXT, NOT A LIVE DEFECT.** Asked on hardware as an A/B/A rather than read off the diff: slot 8 set to **Gallagher → Indala → Gallagher**, and the PWM base clock follows the type every time — **125kHz → 1MHz → 125kHz** — while `have pwm seq` stays **True** across all three and `frames per burst` recomputes **21 → 31 → 21**. Both halves of the old interlock are therefore dead: `lf_sense_disable()` no longer nulls the sequence (C129) and `pwm_reinit_if_clock_changed()` re-inits on the type change rather than only at sense-enable (C130). ⇒ **No `FIXES.md` entry** — there is no upstream defect left to carry a PR, only a docstring that outlived the bug it warned about, and the warning is corrected in place. ⚠ Worth stating because the stale line was about to be read as a live constraint on how emulate tests may be sequenced, which would have cost a power cycle per arm for nothing | 3 type changes, A/B/A, 3 emudebug dumps | ⭐ A/B/A rather than A/B: returning to Gallagher proves the clock tracks the type in both directions | L342 |
| C376 | ⛔⛔ **FIXING `flipper.py` WAS NOT ENOUGH — `emugrade.sh` RE-MANUFACTURED THE SAME BLIND RESULT ONE LAYER UP, AND I PROVED IT ON THE DEAD BENCH RATHER THAN ASSUMING IT.** C374 made the reader script abort on a refused plugin load. But `emugrade.sh` captured it as `o=$(fread)` with `2>&1` and grepped for `psk N/N` — which **discards both the exit status and the abort message**. Run against the known-dead Flipper it printed **`✓ null before clean`**, `gallagher  expected ask   psk -   ask -`, and **`✓ null after clean`**: a textbook A/B/A negative, with the abort text swallowed into a variable nothing examined. ⇒ That is C373 regenerating itself on the next hands session, from a script written to encode C328's discipline. ⭐ **Fixed and verified against the same dead bench**: `fread` returns 3 when the instrument refuses, `null_check` and the arm loop both abort through `reader_dead`, and the run now stops at the FIRST null with the cause and the remedy named — before a single arm is scored. ⚠ **The lesson is about layers, not about this script**: a guard added at the bottom of a stack protects nothing unless every caller propagates it, and shell's `$(...)` silently drops exactly what the guard produces. ⭐ A clean `fread` is now the positive control for rig A, which is the only one available — a silent reader and a silent emulator produce identical numbers | 1 run against a known-dead reader before the fix, 1 after | ⭐ running the wrapper against the STILL-BROKEN bench, instead of fixing the leaf and declaring the class closed | L343 |
| C377 | ⭐⭐⭐ **THE FLIPPER'S `rfid` PLUGIN DIES OF HEAP FRAGMENTATION MID-SESSION, AND THAT ONE FACT EXPLAINS U18, C373 AND EVERY 'INTERMITTENT' EMULATE RESULT THIS BRANCH HAS RECORDED.** `rfid` is not built in: it is `/ext/apps/RFID/lfrfid.fap`, **66,304 bytes**, and the loader must place it in ONE contiguous block. Measured across this session: **118,304 bytes largest-block at boot → 63,880 after ~12 minutes and six emulate arms — with 107,464 bytes still FREE**. ⇒ **Fragmentation, not exhaustion**: the plugin starts refusing the moment the largest block falls below its own size, and it does so in the MIDDLE of a run, with nothing about the bench having changed. ⛔ **This retires my own C373 diagnosis of an API mismatch**, which was wrong and would have sent the operator to rebuild a `.fap` that was never at fault: `uptime` proves the Flipper had **not** rebooted since C353 read 3 of 3 on the same firmware — **10h54m**, matching C355's 00:29:36 boot — so a static ABI mismatch cannot explain a reader that worked and then did not. ✅ **A `power reboot` over the Flipper's own CLI fixes it in ~10 seconds** and needs no hands: largest block back to 118,288. ⭐ **Now automatic**: `flipper.py heap` exits 4 below `.fap + 12000` headroom, `flipper.py reboot` power-cycles and waits, `emugrade.sh` checks before the first arm and retries once behind a reboot if one dies anyway. ⚠ **The tell to remember** is `free` reporting plenty of free heap and a largest block under 66,304 — *free* is the number that misleads here | largest-block measured at 4 points across 2 boots; 2 reboots, 1 proactive and 1 recovering a dead run | ⭐ `uptime` — it separates *the firmware changed* from *the firmware has been running too long*, and it costs one command | L344 |
| C378 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **THE EMULATE COLUMN IS MEASURED FOR THE FIRST TIME: 8 OF 11 PROTOCOLS EMULATE, ALL SIX OF SIX, AND THE THREE THAT DO NOT ARE EXACTLY THE FSK FAMILY.** Every previous number here was void (C373); these are the first real ones. **PSK1 — Indala 6/6, IDTECK 6/6, Keri 6/6, NexWatch 6/6**, each with its ASK arm at 0/6 as the built-in wrong-modulation control. **ASK/biphase — Gallagher 6/6, Securakey 6/6, Noralsy 6/6, GProxII 6/6.** **FSK — HID Prox 0/6, ioProx 0/6, AWID 0/6.** ⭐ **The three failures are bracketed by positives in the same run** — Noralsy 6/6 immediately before and GProxII 6/6 immediately after — so they are a real silence, not a dead reader (which is the failure C373/C376 existed to rule out), and clean nulls sit either side of every batch. ⭐⭐ **CREDENTIALS VERIFIED, NOT JUST HIT COUNTS** — the score alone would repeat C353's front-end trap: GProxII reads back **`GProxII FAC2A38C2B081AF0210B12C2`, FC 123 / Card 1337 / LEN 26 — byte-identical to the raw written** — and Indala reads **`Indala26 CD7A1D30`, FC 52 / Card 63612**, the tag's own credential. ⛔⛔ **THIS REFUTES U12 / C242.** *A held level does not transmit, so GProxII cannot be emulated this way at all* is false: it emulates byte-exact, 6 of 6. ⇒ **The emulate gap is now precisely one family — FSK2a — which is U11, exactly where C217 left it** with AWID silent and a Gallagher control at 6/6. ⚠ Indala renders `Checksum: -` on the Flipper while FC and Card match exactly; noted, not chased | 11 arms x 6 attempts x 2 modes, 3 batches, nulls before and after each; 2 arms re-read for credential | ⭐ the wrong-modulation arm and the bracketing positives are controls the run carries itself, so no arm rests on the reader being healthy at a different time | L344 |
| C379 | ⭐⭐ **U11 IS SCOPED BEFORE A LINE OF IT IS WRITTEN: THE FSK LOADER IS FINE, SO THE FAULT IS IN THE EMITTER.** The free discriminator U18 lacked for a whole unit, asked FIRST this time. All three silent protocols load a sequence — HID Prox (type 200) **`have pwm seq : True`, 125kHz, 14 frames/burst**; ioProx (201) True, 125kHz, **16**; AWID (307) True, 125kHz, **14**. ⭐ The three frames/burst values are **distinct**, so `recompute_frames_per_burst()` walked three real sequences of different total duration rather than returning a default — the modulator ran and produced a waveform for each. ⇒ `lf_tag_data_loadcb_inner()` is NOT where U11 lives, and neither is the clock: **125kHz is correct for a non-PSK1 type**. What is left is the FSK2a ENCODING itself — the ASK emitters put one PWM entry per bit with `counter_top` set to the carrier cycles that bit occupies, and FSK2a needs `counter_top` 8 or 10 per TONE PERIOD instead. ⚠ These three are the only 0/6 arms in a run where Noralsy scored 6/6 immediately before and GProxII 6/6 immediately after (C378), so the silence is a real emitter fault and not an instrument one. ⇒ Next step is a `ctest/roundtrip.c` arm per protocol, which is where three wrong encodings were caught before (C156) | 3 FSK types configured and dumped | ⭐ distinct frames/burst — a default would have been identical across all three, so one number separates *loaded* from *skipped* | L345 |
| C380 | ⚠ **C226'S FIXED-MARK CORRECTION WAS APPLIED TO AWID ONLY — HID PROX AND ioPROX STILL EMITTED `counter_top / 2`. FIXED, FLASHED, AND IT DOES NOT RESTORE EMULATION.** C226 measured a real FSK2a emission on this bench: the HIGH run is **4 carrier cycles on every tone**, and only the LOW run varies to carry the frequency — the mark is the tag's load-modulation pulse, a property of the modulator, not of the tone. AWID was corrected and pinned by `trial_awid_duty()`; **`hidprox.c` and `ioprox.c` were never touched** and emitted a mark of 4 for RF/8 but **5** for the long tone. ⛔ **No round trip in `ctest` can catch this** — our demodulator only ever looks at the tone's PERIOD, which is identical either way — and `hidprox.c` is in none of ctest's arms at all (M41). ✅ Both now use a named `LF_FSK2a_PWM_MARK_CYCLES`, built and flashed to #1 (`v2.2.0-696-g180a44f`). ⛔⛔ **AND THE ARMS ARE STILL 0 OF 6** — HID Prox, ioProx and AWID all silent, with **Gallagher 6/6 immediately before AND after** in the same run and clean nulls either side. ⇒ **The mark shape is NOT the cause of the FSK silence**, and this entry is a correctness fix that narrows U11 rather than closing it. ⭐ **What U11 has now eliminated, each by measurement**: the loader (C379, `have pwm seq` True), playback (36 bursts per read against Gallagher's 12, the ratio being read duration not emission), the frame arithmetic, our own decoder (round-trips exact), and now the mark. ⇒ **What is left is C217's original question and it is still unanswered — does the PWM peripheral actually emit what it is asked at `counter_top` 8 and 10, where every working emitter here uses 32, 40 or 64?** ⚠ An FSK tone at fc/8 switches the modulator at 15.6 kHz against ASK RF/32's 3.9 kHz, so a 4x-faster switch through the same analog path is a live hypothesis and is NOT yet tested. ⇒ Next step is to CAPTURE the emission — the Flipper's `rfid raw_read` writes samples to a file that can be pulled over its CLI — and compare a silent HID emission against a working Gallagher one | 3 FSK arms x 6 attempts x 2 modes, bracketed by 2 Gallagher controls at 6/6, nulls either side; 1 firmware build + flash | ⭐ the bracketing control either side of the failures, which is what makes this a real negative rather than another dead-reader artefact | L346 |
| C381 | ⭐⭐⭐ **THE FSK FAULT IS ISOLATED TO OUR EMITTER: THE AIR GAP AND OUR READER BOTH CARRY fc/8 PERFECTLY.** Ran rig A BACKWARDS — the Flipper emulating `H10301 7B11D7` and our Chameleon reading it — and our reader returns **FC 123 / CN 4567 exactly**, with a clean `LF tag not found` null immediately before. ⇒ Everything shared by the two directions is exonerated in one measurement: the **antenna coupling**, the **distance and geometry**, the **125 kHz carrier**, **FSK2a at fc/8 and fc/10 as a modulation over this gap**, and our **reader and decoder**. ⛔ The only thing left on the failing path that is not on the working one is **our own emitter** — the PWM peripheral and the analog modulation path it drives. ⭐ This is the C217 question narrowed from *is it the emitter or the reader or the bench* to *it is the emitter*, and it cost one command in the direction nobody had tried. ⚠ Note what it does NOT say: our emitter's waveform is correct by every host check available (round-trips through our own decoder exactly, and the mark now matches C226's measured real emission per C380), so the defect is in what the hardware DOES with that waveform, not in the numbers we hand it. ⇒ The live hypothesis is `counter_top` **8 and 10** — every emitter that works here uses **32, 40 or 64**, and an fc/8 tone switches the modulator at **15.6 kHz** against ASK RF/32's 3.9 kHz. ⇒ To settle it the emission must be CAPTURED, and the only instrument on rig A that can hear us is the Flipper's `rfid raw_read` | 1 null + 4 reads of a Flipper FSK emulation | ⭐ running the rig in the untried direction — it exonerates every shared element at once, which no amount of probing the failing direction can do | L347 |
| C382 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **U11'S ROOT CAUSE, MEASURED OFF THE AIR: OUR FSK EMITTERS EMIT ONLY ONE TONE — THERE IS NO FREQUENCY MODULATION AT ALL.** Captured our own emission with the Flipper's `rfid raw_read` and histogrammed the periods `raw_analyze` prints. **HID Prox: 2257 periods in the RF/8 band (58-70 us), ZERO in the RF/10 band (74-88). AWID: 3620 in the RF/8 band, TWO in the RF/10 band.** The scale is pinned by the control in the same run — Gallagher's periods land at 131 and 253 against RF/32's 128 and 256 us half-bits, so one unit is ~1 us and a 64-vs-80 difference is far above the instrument's resolution. ⇒ **An FSK decoder handed a constant tone has no data to find**, which is exactly the `Protocol: not found` both captures return while the Gallagher control decodes byte-exact from the same pipeline (`Protocol: Gallagher [12 00 10 E1 00 00 1A 85]`). ⭐⭐ **AND IT UNIFIES THE WHOLE COLUMN.** Every emitter that WORKS uses a single constant `counter_top` for its entire sequence — Gallagher 32, Securakey 40, Noralsy 32, GProxII one constant, em410x 64, and every PSK1 protocol through psk1.c's `LF_PSK1_SUBCARRIER_TOP`. **The three FSK emitters are the ONLY ones that VARY `counter_top` within a sequence, and varying is precisely what does not reach the air.** ⇒ The leading mechanism is that the **per-entry `counter_top` of a WAVEFORM-mode PWM sequence is not being applied**, so the whole sequence plays at one period. ⚠ **That mechanism is an inference; the single tone is the measurement.** The confirming test is named and cheap: set BOTH of hidprox's tones to `counter_top` 10, reflash, and re-capture — if the emission moves to ~80 us the value is honoured when constant and the defect is specifically the variation. ⛔ This also explains why no host test could ever find it: `ctest` decodes the SEQUENCE ARRAY, where the two tones differ correctly, not the emission | 3 captures + 1 control decoded, 6538 periods histogrammed, scale pinned by the control | ⭐ capturing our own emission — the first time anything on this bench has measured what we transmit rather than what we intended | L348 |
| C383 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **CONFIRMED BY THE NAMED TEST: `counter_top` IS HONOURED WHEN CONSTANT AND IGNORED WHEN IT VARIES. U11 HAS A MEASURED ROOT CAUSE AND A DESIGNED FIX.** C382 inferred the mechanism; this ran the experiment it named. Both hidprox tones were forced to `counter_top` 10, built, flashed to #1, and the emission re-captured: **periods near RF/10 went 0 → 2640 and periods near RF/8 went 2257 → 4**, with the histogram's peak moving from 63 to **79 us**. ⇒ The peripheral plays exactly the period it is given — **the whole sequence at ONE period** — so the defect is specifically that a per-entry `counter_top` which VARIES within a sequence is not applied. That is now measurement, not inference, and it explains why every working emitter here uses a single constant value and only the three FSK ones do not. ⭐⭐ **THE FIX FOLLOWS FROM `gcd(8, 10) = 2`**: express both tones on a CONSTANT `counter_top` of 2 carrier cycles and carry the frequency in the DUTY pattern instead — RF/8 becomes `on, on, off, off` (mark 4, gap 4) and RF/10 becomes `on, on, off, off, off` (mark 4, gap 6), which also preserves C226/C380's measured fixed 4-cycle mark for free. ⚠ **Cost is entries**: ~25 entries per bit against today's 5-6, so a 96-bit HID frame needs ~2,400 entries (~19 KB) where the array is sized 576 today. Precedent exists — the PSK1 path already walks 3,584 entries for a 224-bit frame — but the RAM must be budgeted before this is written, and ioProx's long tone is 11, so `gcd(8, 11) = 1` and it needs `counter_top` 1 or a corrected tone. ⛔ Do not write the emitter until that budget is on paper | 1 diagnostic build + flash, 1 re-capture, 2857 periods; bench restored to the committed build `v2.2.0-702-g5987a8c` | ⭐ forcing the varying value to be CONSTANT — it separates *the value is wrong* from *the variation is not applied*, which no capture of the failing case alone could do | L349 |
| C384 | ⭐⭐ **THE RAM BUDGET C383 GATED U11 ON, TAKEN OFF THE MAP FILE RATHER THAN ESTIMATED — AND IT DECIDES THE DESIGN.** From `objects/application.map`: the RAM region is **218,392 B**, `.data + .bss` is **126,644 B**, leaving **91,748 B** for heap and stack. The three FSK sequence buffers are **0x1200 = 4,608 B each, 13,824 B total** (all eleven LF PWM buffers together are ~24 KB). ⇒ Under C383's constant-`counter_top` scheme at 8 B per entry: **HID 2,400 entries = 19,200 B**, **AWID the same**, **ioProx 4,224 = 33,792 B** at its current long tone of 11 (`gcd(8,11) = 1` forces `counter_top` 1) or **2,048 = 16,384 B** if that tone were 10. ⛔ **Keeping three private buffers costs +58,368 B — 64% of all remaining RAM. That is refused.** ✅ **One SHARED buffer is the design**: only one tag type is ever loaded at a time (`lf_tag_data_loadcb_inner()` sets exactly one `m_tag_type` and one `m_pwm_seq`), so the three can never be live together. Sized for the worst case it costs **+19,968 B (21.8% of free)** with ioProx at 11, or **+5,376 B (5.9%)** at 10. ⭐ **F5 is the precedent** — it fixed exactly this shape, two 28 KB capture buffers resident at once. ⚠ **ioProx's long tone of 11 is now a question worth measuring** (the Proxmark uses fc/10 there, and 6 x 11 = 66 against a 64-cycle bit) but it is NOT assumed: the shared buffer works either way and the tone must be settled by a capture, not by reading another tool's constant | map file: 1 RAM region, 2 section sizes, 11 buffer sizes; 4 entry-count derivations | ⭐ the map file — the arrays' real sizes and the real headroom, where an estimate of either would have decided this wrongly | L350 |
| C385 | ⛔⛔ **C384'S FIX IS ILLEGAL AS WRITTEN — `counter_top = 2` IS BELOW THE HARDWARE MINIMUM — AND THE CORRECTED SCHEME NEEDS A CLOCK CHANGE.** `psk1.h` records the constraint and I had read past it: **COUNTERTOP in WaveForm mode has a minimum valid value of 3** (Nordic PS). C384 derived `gcd(8, 10) = 2` and proposed exactly that, so the design I committed could not have been built. ⭐ **The repair is the BASE CLOCK, not the arithmetic.** At the FSK types' current **125 kHz** clock one tick is one carrier cycle and the tone periods are 8 and 10 ticks — gcd 2, illegal. At **1 MHz** (what every PSK1 type already uses) one tick is 1 us, a carrier cycle is 8 ticks, and the tones are **64 and 80 ticks — `gcd = 16`, comfortably legal**. ⇒ **Constant `counter_top` 16**: RF/8 is 4 entries (2 on, 2 off) and RF/10 is 5 (2 on, 3 off), which reproduces C226/C380's measured **4-carrier-cycle mark = 32 us = exactly 2 entries** for free. ⭐ ioProx's tone of 11 also becomes legal: 64 and 88 ticks, `gcd = 8`, mark 4 entries — so the tone-11 question stays open rather than blocking. ✅ **C384's RAM budget survives untouched** because the entry counts are identical — HID and AWID 2,400 entries (19,200 B), ioProx 4,224 (33,792 B) — so one shared buffer remains the design. ⚠ **The new cost is a clock generalisation**: `IS_PSK1_TYPE` currently gates the 1 MHz choice in `pwm_init()`, `pwm_reinit_if_clock_changed()` AND `recompute_frames_per_burst()`'s `hz`, and all three must move to one *what clock does this type need* helper — miss the third and frames-per-burst is 8x wrong. ⇒ **Nothing was built on the bad design**; it was caught by reading the header of the module being copied, before a line was written | 1 constraint recovered from `psk1.h`, 2 gcd derivations, 3 call sites identified | ⭐ reading the house module's header rather than only its code — the constraint was written down, in the file, by someone who had already hit it | L351 |
| C386 | ⛔⛔ **THE CONSTANT-`counter_top` EMITTER IS BUILT, CORRECT BY EVERY HOST AND DEVICE CHECK — AND THE AIR IS STILL SINGLE-TONED. C383'S MECHANISM IS NARROWER THAN I STATED.** Shipped `lf/utils/fsk2a_mod.c`: one shared buffer, constant `counter_top` 16 at a 1 MHz clock, the tone carried by the duty pattern; HID and AWID rewired to it; `IS_1MHZ_PWM_TYPE` added so the clock choice is ONE edit across the three sites C385 named. ✅ **Verified at every layer.** RAM matches C384's prediction to the byte — shared buffer `0x4b00` = 19,200 B, `.bss` **+9,984 B**, the two private buffers gone. On the device: **clock 1 MHz**, `have pwm seq` True, **`SEQ[0].CNT` 9340 = 2335 entries**, and that count arithmetically requires **31 one-bits**, so both tone shapes ARE in the sequence. ⭐⭐ **And ctest caught a HARNESS defect on the way**: `render_ask()` hard-codes half the period high and ignores the duty field entirely, so it rendered a correct emitter as `NO DECODE` — a confident red from the instrument, the C374 shape again. Added `render_level_div()`, which honours the duty AND divides 1 MHz ticks down to carrier-cycle samples (every other emitter runs at 125 kHz where the two coincide, which is why no renderer needed it before). AWID then decodes **exact**, and the rewritten duty pin reports **543 tones, mark fixed at 4 cycles, gaps 378 x4 + 165 x6** — *the same counts the old encoding was pinned at*, so the physical shape is unchanged and still matches C226's measured real emission. ⛔⛔ **AND THE EMISSION IS STILL ONE TONE: HID 1183 periods in the RF/8 band against 16 in the RF/10 band, and 0/6 on the Flipper with Gallagher 6/6 as the control in the same run.** ⇒ **So it is NOT simply *a varying `counter_top` is not applied*** — a CONSTANT `counter_top` whose tone lives in the duty is single-toned too. What both encodings share is that the long tone needs a LONGER LOW period, and that is precisely what never appears. ⚠ **No regression**: Gallagher 6/6 before and after, ctest fully green, gate clean. ⇒ **The next step needs an instrument we control.** The Flipper's raw reader is a black box we are inferring from; `rdrcap.py` + `DATA_CMD_LF_READER_CAPTURE` hand back OUR OWN undecoded samples, and that needs the two Chameleons facing each other — the standing bench request in `NEXT.md`, which now has a concrete question to answer | 1 module + 2 protocols rewired, 1 build + flash, map-file RAM check, 1 device dump, 2 ctest arms fixed, 1 capture, 1 graded run with control | ⭐ the entry count on the device — 2335 entries pins the bit mix, so the sequence's contents are known without dumping it | L352 |
| C387 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **U11'S FAILURE IS RATE-DEPENDENT, NOT AN ENCODING ERROR: THE EMITTER CAN PRODUCE EITHER TONE PERFECTLY AND LOSES THE LONG ONE IN PROPORTION TO HOW OFTEN THE TONE CHANGES.** Three diagnostic frames through the same emitter and the same instrument: **uniform long (all ones) — 3031 periods in the RF/10 band against ONE in RF/8**; **alternating every 4 bits (`0xF0`) — 2459 RF/8 against 261 RF/10**; **alternating every bit (`0xAA`, 48 ones and 48 zeros evenly interleaved) — 1078 RF/8 against ZERO RF/10**. ⇒ The encoding, the PWM and the 2-on/3-off duty pattern are all CORRECT — a steady RF/10 comes out at 79 us exactly as designed. What degrades is the TRANSITION between tones, monotonically with its frequency. ⭐ The mark quality tracks the same curve: ~28-33 us against an intended 32 when the tone changes every 4 bits, collapsing to 7-25 us when it changes every bit. ⇒ That is a settling or transient signature, not a digital one. ⭐⭐ **AND IT IS NOT AN INSTRUMENT ARTEFACT — THE CONTROL WAS TAKEN.** A genuine mixed-tone FSK reference (the Flipper emulating `H10301`) captured through OUR OWN reader shows **both bands present, 389 near RF/8 and 79 near RF/10**, so a mixed-tone emission is measurable on this bench and our 1078-to-0 is a real defect. ⚠ **What this does NOT establish** is the cause. A resonant tank needing time to settle when the modulation period changes fits every number here, but so would several other things, and a real FSK tag alternates every bit and works — it shorts its coil, a far larger and faster perturbation than driving a transistor across the same node. ⇒ **Next is modulation DEPTH, not another encoding**: if the emitter cannot move the tank far enough to re-establish a new tone within one tone period, more depth is the lever and a third encoding is not. ⛔ C386's *the long tone never appears* is superseded — it appears perfectly when it is not asked to alternate | 3 diagnostic builds + flashes + captures, 1 reference capture through our own reader; bench restored to `v2.2.0-710-g4d3a27d` on both units | ⭐ sweeping the ALTERNATION RATE rather than the encoding — it turns a binary works/does-not into a monotonic curve, which names the mechanism's family | L353 |
| C388 | ⛔ **MODULATION DEPTH IS REFUTED AS THE LEVER — MORE MARK IS WORSE, AND C226'S MEASURED SHAPE IS ALREADY THE BEST ONE.** C387 named depth as the next thing to try, so it was tried as a single clean variable: `LF_FSK2A_MARK_CYCLES` 4 -> 6 with `counter_top` untouched, against the known worst case (the `0xAA` alternating frame, which scores 1078 short and ZERO long at mark 4). At mark 6 the emission **collapses**: 38 periods near RF/8, still zero near RF/10, and a capture half the size. The mark histogram splits into ~556 us held levels and ~10 us blips rather than a tone at all. ⇒ **The reason is arithmetic and should have been predicted**: a 6-cycle mark is **75% duty on the RF/8 tone** and 60% on RF/10, so the field is loaded most of the time and the CONTRAST the reader needs is what gets spent. ⭐ That is a second independent vote for C226's measured reference — a fixed 4-cycle mark is not just *what a real tag does*, it is at or near the optimum for the thing a reader actually measures. ⇒ **The lever list for U11 is now empty of things reachable from this bench**: the encoding is correct (either tone emits perfectly on its own), the loader, playback, clock, frame arithmetic, decoder, mark shape and depth are all eliminated by measurement, and what remains is the TRANSITION between tones, which degrades monotonically with its rate (C387). ⇒ **This is where U11 genuinely needs hands**, and unlike C377's mistaken request it is backed by a rate curve and a control: our own capture instrument (`rdrcap.py`) must hear our own emission, which needs the two Chameleons facing each other, or a scope on LF_OA | 1 diagnostic build + flash + capture at mark 6, against the mark-4 baseline; bench restored to `v2.2.0-712-gba7e722` | ⭐ trying the named lever immediately rather than reasoning about it — it cost one build and removed the last cheap hypothesis | L354 |
| C389 | ⭐⭐ **FIXED THE INSTRUMENT LINE THAT COST U18 A WHOLE UNIT — IT WAS STILL THERE, STILL LYING, AND I HAD READ IT AGAIN THIS TICK WITHOUT NOTICING.** `hw lfdebug` printed **`⛔ PWM0 IS NOT READING OUR SEQUENCE`** unconditionally whenever `SEQ[0].PTR` did not match. But *ours* there means the **READER's** array: `lf_tag_em.c` drives the SAME peripheral, PWM0, with its own sequence, so while the device is emulating **that banner is the CORRECT and expected state**, not a fault. ⛔ It is what U18 read as *the emulator never sets `m_pwm_seq`* — for a whole unit — while `hw emudebug` said `have pwm seq: True` and had never been asked (C373). ⚠ **And it caught me again this tick**: the shared FSK buffer dump printed `ours: False` beside the address `0x2000DA1C`, which IS our buffer; I noticed the contradiction, moved on, and only came back to it afterwards. ⇒ **Fixed to name whose array is meant and to alarm only when the READER should own the peripheral**: the line now reads `SEQ[0].PTR reader`, and when `reader_inited` is false it says *expected while emulating — PWM0 belongs to the tag path; ask hw emudebug*, naming the command that actually answers. ✅ Verified both ways on hardware: emulating gives the explanatory note and no alarm, reading gives `reader_inited True` with the pointer matching. ⭐ **The general rule**: an instrument shared between two owners must say WHICH owner it is reporting on, or its most alarming output is its least trustworthy — and a ⛔ that fires in a normal state trains the reader to discount every ⛔ | 1 CLI fix, verified in both device modes | ⭐ noticing that the address printed beside *False* was our own buffer — the contradiction was on screen and is what makes this a fix rather than a guess | L355 |
| C390 | ⭐⭐ **U13 DONE — §9h RE-VERIFIED AGAINST THE BRANCH, AND THE GATE'S MEASURED COST HAS DOUBLED SINCE IT WAS WRITTEN.** Re-measured with `arm-none-eabi-size` on both builds rather than trusting the table: gated **off** text **335,492** bss **146,068**; gated **on** text **337,052** bss **154,084**. ⇒ **1,560 bytes of flash and 8,016 of RAM**, against the 1,088 and 4,008 the section claimed. ⚠ **The RAM cost is EXACTLY double** — 4,008 → 8,016 — which is too clean to be drift; it is two staging buffers where there was one. ⛔ Those old figures were a load-bearing claim for a PR and would have been quoted to a reviewer. ⭐ **Every line number in §9h's site table had also drifted** (`data_cmd.h` 235/236/264 → 258/259/292, dispatch rows 3856/3857/3853 → 3968/3969/3961, `chameleon_cli_unit.py` 9029/9055 → 9321/9348), so the table now carries a warning that the line numbers are a convenience and the CONTRACT is the `#if LF_RESEARCH_CMDS_ENABLED` blocks plus the one `-D` — grep for the macro. The gate's own site, `application/Makefile:431`, was missing from the table entirely and is added. ✅ **The substance was all correct**: one macro, default 0, set to 1 by this branch's Makefile, four gated sites, and `rdrcap.py` needing nothing. ⚠ **I nearly filed a false finding on the way** — an early grep with `head -3` truncated the Makefile hit and made the gate look unset; the fix was to re-run it, not to report it. ⭐ And `./checkdocs.sh` caught this entry being CITED before it was written, which is the cross-reference check earning its keep on its own author | 2 builds measured, 14 line numbers re-verified, 1 missing site added | ⭐ re-measuring rather than re-reading — the section's prose was right and both its numbers were wrong, and only one of those is visible from the text | L356 |
| C391 | ⭐⭐ **U11'S HANDS WINDOW IS NOW ONE COMMAND, AND THE INSTRUMENT IS VALIDATED BEFORE THE BENCH MOVES.** The bench was asked rather than assumed — **#2 cannot hear #1 emulating Gallagher**, a protocol that emulates 6/6 (C378), so the two Chameleons are still not facing each other and U11 stays blocked. ⭐ Rather than wait idle, the whole measurement is written and tested against that negative: `./fskcap.sh` runs the coupling check, then captures each FSK arm through `rdrcap.py` and histograms it with the new `./tonehist.py`. ⛔ **The coupling check uses Gallagher ON PURPOSE** — a null from a proven-6/6 arm means NOT COUPLED rather than *the emitter is broken*, which is exactly the distinction a silent reader destroys and which cost this branch a whole unit (C373/C374/C376). The script **aborts** there and says so in bench terms; verified by running it right now, uncoupled. ⭐⭐ **`tonehist.py` is validated against a known-good reference BEFORE it is trusted**: the Flipper's own mixed-tone `H10301`, captured through our reader, gives **389 periods near RF/8 and 94 near RF/10 — both bands present**, which is what a working FSK emission looks like in our own sample domain. ⭐ **And the units need no calibration**: the SAADC is PPI-triggered from the carrier PWM, one sample per carrier cycle, so an RF/8 tone is literally **8 samples** and RF/10 is **10** — no microseconds, no scale to pin, unlike every Flipper-derived number so far. ⚠ The tool reports its own noise floor (2,773 sub-6-sample runs against 669 tones on the reference) rather than hiding it, because a histogram that conceals its floor is how a null gets read as a measurement (F05) | 1 coupling probe with a proven-positive arm, 1 analyzer validated on a known-good reference, 1 abort path exercised | ⭐ testing the harness against the FAILING bench state first — the abort path is the one that runs when something is wrong, and it is the one usually never exercised | L357 |
| C392 | ⭐⭐ **ioPROX'S LONG TONE OF 11 IS INCONSISTENT WITH OUR OWN DECODER, WHICH HAS NO fc/11 AT ALL — THE DEFERRED QUESTION RESOLVES TOWARD 10, AND THE CHEAPER BUFFER STANDS.** C385 left ioProx's `LF_FSK2a_PWM_LO_FREQ_TOP_VALUE = 11` open as *measure, do not assume*, because it decides a **16,384 B** buffer against a **33,792 B** one (`gcd(64,80)=16` vs `gcd(64,88)=8`). ⭐ **The answer is in our own shipping code**: `fskdemod.c`, the demodulator ioProx's codec allocates, discriminates exactly two bins — **`GOERTZEL(15625.0f, 125000.0f)` = fc/8 and `GOERTZEL(12500.0f, 125000.0f)` = fc/10**. There is no fc/11 anywhere in the reader, and our ioProx READ arm is verified 4/4 against tags the Proxmark's own encoder wrote — so the fc/10 bin is validated against real tags while the emitter's 11 matches nothing. ⇒ **An ioProx frame we emit at fc/11 could not be decoded by our own reader**, which is a self-inconsistency independent of anything the Flipper does. ⇒ C384's cheaper option holds: **one shared 19,200 B buffer**, `counter_top` 16 for all three FSK protocols. ⛔ **THE CONSTANT IS DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED YET.** The ioProx emitter scores 0/6 and cannot be verified on air until the bench moves (§5); changing a constant that cannot be tested is how this branch has repeatedly acquired plausible wrong values. Change it in the same window that measures it. ⚠ **AND THE DIRECT MEASUREMENT FAILED, WHICH IS WHY THIS RESTS ON THE SOURCE.** A real ioProx tag was written by pm3 (`XSF(01)53:01337, Raw 007854e03059cdf7 ( ok )`, verified twice) and captured through our own reader, but `tonehist.py` could not separate 10 from 11: the histogram decays monotonically (10:68, 11:24, 12:27) against a **2,264-run noise floor**, with no second peak. The same tool resolves the Flipper's mixed-tone reference cleanly, so this is a marginal capture rather than a broken instrument — but it does NOT vote either way, and is not quoted as if it did. ✅ Bench restored: the T5577 is back to HID H10301 FC 123 / CN 4567, verified by pm3 (`parity ( ok )`) and by our own reader | 1 pm3 write verified twice, 1 capture, 2 hypotheses tested, 1 source audit, 1 restore verified two ways | ⭐ the source: a decoder that reads real tags is a measurement someone already took, and it outranks a marginal capture of mine | L358 |
| C393 | ⭐⭐ **ALL ELEVEN `FIXES.md` ENTRIES ARE NOW REGRESSION-TESTED — THE LAST TWO WERE ONLY EVER BLOCKED BY A READER THAT COULD NOT START.** C351 left F10 and F11 reporting NOT CHECKED because both are EMULATION fixes needing the Flipper, whose `rfid` plugin would not load (C377). That is fixed and automatic, so `./fixcheck.sh` now measures them: **11 pass, 0 FAIL, 0 not checkable**. ⭐ **F10** — *changing a slot's LF type silently disarms emulation until a reboot* — is tested by the strongest pair available: **Gallagher → Indala with no power cycle, both emulating 4/4**, which also crosses the PWM base-clock boundary (125 kHz → 1 MHz), the other half of what that fix had to get right. ⭐ **F11** — *the burst is a fixed FRAME COUNT, so long-window readers fail at the boundary* — is tested by the one tell no read score reveals: the two protocols must report **DIFFERENT** frames-per-burst, and they do, **Gallagher 21 against Indala 31**. A regression to a constant makes them equal while every arm still reads. ⛔⛔ **AND IT TOOK TWO OF MY OWN HARNESS BUGS TO GET THERE, THE FIRST OF WHICH PRINTED A FALSE REGRESSION.** `SLOT=9` — the device has **eight** slots, so every econfig was refused by the argument parser, the device kept whatever was already loaded, and the run reported **`⛔ F11 REGRESSED — both protocols report the same frames-per-burst`**. Then a grep that matched only `ASK 3/4` and not the single-mode `ASK: 3/4` scored every arm as a miss and reported *neither arm scored*. ⇒ **Neither was written down as a firmware verdict**, because the rig-A checks degrade to NOT CHECKED rather than FAIL when an arm is silent — the same principle that made this script report its own gaps. ⚠ **The F11 line was one commit away from claiming an upstream fix had regressed**, which is the most expensive kind of false finding this register could produce | 11 entries scored, 2 harness bugs found and fixed, 1 false regression caught before it was recorded | ⭐ suspecting the harness first when a rig-A arm fails — the two bugs were mine and the firmware was right both times | L359 |
| C394 | ✅ **THE EIGHT WORKING EMULATE ARMS ARE RE-VERIFIED ON THE CURRENT BUILD — MY OWN CHANGES REGRESSED NOTHING.** C378 measured 8 of 11 at 6/6, but that was BEFORE C386 rewired the emulation path underneath every protocol: the PWM base-clock choice moved from `IS_PSK1_TYPE` to `IS_1MHZ_PWM_TYPE` at **three** call sites including `recompute_frames_per_burst()`'s tick-to-microsecond conversion, HID and AWID moved to a shared waveform buffer, and two private buffers were deleted for a net `.bss` change of **+9,984 B**. ⚠ **Those touch `pwm_init()` and the burst arithmetic for TYPES THAT WERE ALREADY WORKING**, so *the arms I did not change are still fine* was an assumption, not a measurement — and a wrong base clock is silent on the air rather than loud (C130). ⇒ Re-graded all eight: **PSK1 Indala, IDTECK, Keri, NexWatch 6/6 each with their ASK arm at 0/6; ASK/biphase Gallagher, Securakey, Noralsy, GProxII 6/6 each with their PSK arm at 0/6**, clean nulls either side of both batches. ⇒ **No regression**, and the wrong-modulation controls confirm it is the right protocol being read rather than a front end hitting anything. ⭐ Worth doing rather than assuming precisely because the change was shared infrastructure: the three FSK arms it was written for still score 0/6, so the edit had no visible effect anywhere — which is exactly the shape of a change that could have broken something quietly | 8 arms x 6 attempts x 2 modes in 2 batches, nulls before and after each | ⭐ re-running the arms I did NOT touch — the ones I touched were always going to be checked, and the shared path is what actually carried the risk | L360 |
| C395 | ⭐⭐ **`FIXES.md` GAINS ITS FIRST UNFIXED ENTRY, DELIBERATELY: F12, FSK2a EMULATION EMITS A CONSTANT TONE.** Three shipping protocols — HID Prox, ioProx and AWID — expose an emulate path that puts **no data on the air at all**, while the other eight score 6 of 6 (C378). ⇒ **A register of found defects that silently omits the UNREPAIRED ones tells a maintainer the opposite of the truth**, and two prior audits (C349, C350) had already established that this file's failure mode is incompleteness. ⭐ The entry carries the elimination work, which is the part a maintainer cannot redo cheaply because it took a bench, a rate sweep and a positive control: the loader, playback, frame arithmetic, our own decoder, the tone encoding, the mark shape and modulation depth are each ruled out by measurement, and the capture table shows the emitter produces EITHER tone perfectly while losing the long one in proportion to how often it changes. ⚠ It says plainly that the CAUSE is not established and that it is **not a PR**. ⛔ It gets **no `fixcheck.sh` arm** — an unfixed defect has nothing to regress — so the script NAMES it as open instead and the summary line now reads *11 pass, 0 FAIL, 0 not checkable; 1 registered defect still OPEN*. ⭐ **Also corrected a stale claim in the same file**: its header still said *9 of 11 pass, F10 and F11 NOT CHECKED*, which C393 had superseded hours earlier — the header of a register is exactly where a stale number does the most damage | 1 entry added with a 4-row capture table, 1 stale coverage claim corrected, 1 script summary reworked | ⭐ asking what the FILE claims rather than what the entries claim — the header was wrong while every row in it was right | L361 |
| C396 | ⭐⭐ **THE SESSION'S REUSABLE METHODS MOVED OUT OF CLAIM TEXT AND INTO `METHOD.md` — M47 TO M51.** Fourteen of this run's findings were my own tooling rather than the firmware, and the lessons were sitting in individual claims where a fresh context would have to read the whole ledger to find them. `METHOD.md` is what actually gets read. ⇒ **M47** an instrument shared by two owners must NAME which owner it reports on — `hw lfdebug`'s ⛔ fired in a normal state, cost U18 a whole unit, and caught me a second time (C373, C389); a ⛔ that fires normally trains the reader to discount every ⛔. **M48** sweep the RATE, not the encoding — four re-encodings each answered yes/no, while one sweep of how often the tone changes gave 3031/261/0 and named the mechanism's family (C387). **M49** a guard at the bottom of a stack protects nothing unless every caller propagates it — shell's `$(...)` silently drops exactly what a guard produces, so test the WRAPPER against the still-broken state (C376). **M50** validate an analyzer on a known-good reference and exercise the ABORT path, because that branch runs precisely when nobody can notice it is itself broken (C391). **M51** re-run the arms you did NOT change when you change what they stand on. ⭐⭐ **M51 is written as the explicit COMPLEMENT of M39, not a contradiction of it**, and the distinction is causal reach: M39 refuses a regression run with no path from the change to the arm, while C394 had a path that was invisible — a shared clock macro under protocols nobody edited, where a wrong base clock is SILENT rather than loud (C130). ⚠ Leaving that unstated would have left two rules that look opposed | 5 methods added, 1 cross-rule tension resolved | ⭐ writing the new rule against the existing one it appears to contradict — the conflict is the reader's first objection and answering it is what makes the rule usable | L362 |
| C397 | ⭐⭐ **`checkdocs.sh` GAINS A FIFTH DRIFT CLASS — STALE COUNTS — AND THE BREAK TEST CAUGHT MY FIRST VERSION NOT CATCHING THE THING IT WAS WRITTEN FOR.** The four existing checks ask whether a REFERENCE resolves, and neither of the day's two worst notes defects was a broken reference: §9h claimed the instrumentation gate cost 1,088 flash / 4,008 RAM when re-measurement said 1,560 / **8,016 — the RAM figure had exactly doubled** (C390), and FIXES.md's own header still said *9 of 11 pass* hours after C393 made it 11 of 11. Both were load-bearing numbers a maintainer is handed, and both were caught by someone happening to read them. ⭐ **A stale count is not *a claim that is false*, which this file rightly refuses to judge — it is TWO PLACES STATING DIFFERENT NUMBERS FOR THE SAME THING**, which is internal consistency and is mechanically checkable. ⇒ Three narrow checks, each **proven by a deliberate break**: F-numbers must be 1..N with no gaps or repeats; `N of M pass` must agree with the register; and AUTOPILOT's `**N registered**` must agree with it too. ⛔⛔ **THE FIRST VERSION FAILED ITS OWN BREAK TEST.** It compared only the DENOMINATOR, so restoring today's actual stale sentence — `9 of 11 pass` — **passed**: the 11 was right and the 9 was the rot. ⚠ **A check written for a specific failure that does not catch that failure is worse than none, because it is believed.** Both numbers are asserted now, and a genuine shortfall trips it deliberately rather than sitting stale. ⭐ Two more things the build caught before shipping: the check's FIRST run flagged a real ambiguity — *11 of 11 pass* against a 12-row table, correct in meaning and confusing on the page — and **both sides were fixed rather than the checker loosened**, since loosening a checker to silence a true ambiguity is how it stops being believed; and an empty-match artefact made it report `'of  pass'` on perfectly clean notes, which is precisely the cry-wolf failure this file warns about twice | 3 checks, 3 deliberate breaks each caught, 2 self-inflicted defects found before commit | ⭐ break-testing the checker against the ACTUAL historical failure rather than a synthetic one — a synthetic break would have passed my broken first version | L363 |

Putting that exact raw on a T5577 and asking the Proxmark settles it: **`Securakey - len: 26 FC: 0x35 Card: 64169, Raw: 7FCB400001ADEA5344300000`** — 0x35 is 53, and the raw is byte-identical. ⇒ The claim is now earned rather than generalised | 6 arms read back, 1 tag written and judged by pm3 | ⭐ chasing a claim I had WIDENED myself — the precise version was two arms and the summary said eight | L364 |
