The tag is correctly formed — `lf t55xx detect` says **ASK, RF/40**, block 0 `000C8060`, blocks 1-3 exactly the raw. ⭐⭐ **THE RIG IS EXONERATED IN THE SAME MINUTE**: the same device, the same tag position, reads **HID FC 123 / CN 4567** immediately afterwards, so this is protocol-specific and not coupling. ⭐ **And Securakey EMULATION still scores 6/6 this session**, so the protocol's encoder and frame are fine — it is the READ path. ⛔ **This contradicts a headline claim**: `lf securakey read` is recorded as returning this exact raw **6 of 6**, and Securakey is one of C343's nineteen arms at 76 of 76. ⚠ **NOT YET ATTRIBUTED, and the instrument-first rule says do not call it a regression until it is.** Nothing changed this session touches the Securakey read path. Two live candidates: a field/drive marginality specific to ASK **RF/40** — the slowest bit rate any read arm uses, and drive has decided arms before (C210) — or an older regression that went unnoticed because the arm was never re-run against a real tag after the capture engine was rewired. ⇒ **Next tick: sweep drive on `lf securakey read` against this reference tag, then bisect the read path.** ✅ Bench restored to HID H10301 FC 123 / CN 4567, verified by pm3 and by our own reader | 5 reads of ours across 2 tags, 2 pm3 confirmations, 1 config dump, 1 same-minute HID control | ⭐ the HID control taken immediately after on the same tag — it separates *our reader is broken* from *the bench moved* in one command | L364 |
| C400 | ✅⛔ **CLOSED AS NOT REPRODUCIBLE BY C412 — and its bracketing was unsound: BOTH its controls were on the GPIO engine while BOTH failing arms were on the SAADC engine.** Re-tested on real pm3-written tags with the judge verifying every write: Gallagher 6/6, Securakey 6/6, Noralsy 6/6. Original text follows. ⛔⛔⛔ **IT IS NOT SECURAKEY: TWO ASK READ ARMS ARE DOWN, AND THE FAULT IS BOUNDED TO THE BIT-RATE-PARAMETERISED ASK FAMILY.** C399 found `lf securakey read` blind on a tag pm3 reads; this widens and bounds it. On the SAME tag position, the SAME device (#2) and within minutes: **Gallagher (ASK RF/32) 0 of 6** and **Securakey (ASK RF/40) 0 of 5**, both on tags the **Proxmark's own encoder** wrote and both of which pm3 reads perfectly (`GALLAGHER - Region: 1 Facility: 4321 Card No.: 6789 Issue Level: 2`). ⭐⭐ **BUT TWO OTHER ARMS PASS IN THE SAME SESSION AND THEY ARE THE CONTROLS THAT MATTER**: **EM410X reads `EM410X/64: 1234567890` 2 of 2**, which is the SAME GPIO/comparator path the ASK family uses — so that path is alive — and **HID Prox reads FC 123 / CN 4567**, which is the SAADC path. ⇒ **Neither capture engine is down, and the bench is not down.** What fails is specifically ASK at **RF/32 and RF/40**, the two rates that go through the decoder this branch made BIT-RATE PARAMETERISED *because the family does not share one*. ⚠ **The failure is TOTAL, not marginal — 0 of 11 across two protocols** — which argues against the obvious bench explanation, that the faster rates need more SNR than RF/64: a marginal field gives intermittent hits, not silence. ⛔ **THIS CONTRADICTS `19 read arms, 76 of 76` (C343) FOR TWO OF THOSE ARMS**, and §1 now says so. ⚠ **STILL NOT ATTRIBUTED.** Nothing changed this session touches the ASK read path, and the instrument-first rule says do not name a regression until it is measured. ⇒ **Next tick: Noralsy (a THIRD ASK RF/32 point) to confirm the boundary, then bisect the ASK decoder against the history to find when these arms last passed.** ✅ Bench restored to HID H10301 FC 123 / CN 4567, verified by pm3 and by our own reader | 11 failing reads across 2 protocols, 4 passing reads across 2 control protocols, 3 pm3 confirmations | ⭐ EM410X as the control — it shares the FAILING protocols' capture path but not their bit rate, which is what turns *our reader is broken* into a bounded claim | L365 |
| C401 | ⛔⛔⛔ **`tonehist.py` IS NOT A VALID INSTRUMENT, C391'S *VALIDATION* IS RETRACTED, AND TODAY'S `fskcap.sh` NUMBERS ARE VOID.** The operator coupled the two Chameleons, `./fskcap.sh` ran for the first time, and HID came back **411 periods near RF/8 and 200 near RF/10** — which reads as *both tones are present after all* and would have retracted U11. ⛔ **It is an artefact.** The control settles it: #1 emulating **EM410X**, an emission #2 DECODES CORRECTLY in the same minute (`EM410X/64: deadbeef88`), produces the identical decaying histogram — 6:129, 7:112, 8:108, 9:81 ... — **10 periods near RF/32 and ZERO near RF/64**, and the tool prints its own `⛔ ZERO long tones — this is U11's signature` on a signal that is provably clean. ⇒ **An instrument that reports the defect on a known-good input measures nothing.** Every histogram it produced decays monotonically from the 6-sample floor rather than showing PEAKS at the tone lengths; the *bands* were bins of a noise curve. ⭐ **THE MECHANISM IS MY OWN THRESHOLD**: a 64-sample moving mean against a capture where an EM410X half-bit is 32 samples smears exactly the structure it is meant to find, and the AC-coupled baseline (~27-sample time constant, C204) does the rest. ⛔⛔ **C391 CLAIMED THIS TOOL WAS VALIDATED AGAINST A KNOWN-GOOD REFERENCE AND IT WAS NOT.** The Flipper's `H10301` gave *389 near RF/8, 94 near RF/10* and I read two bands; it was the same decaying curve, and I never checked for peak structure. ⚠ **That is M50 — validate on a known-good reference — followed in form and failed in substance**, because I did not state in advance what a PASS would look like. ✅ **WHAT SURVIVES**: U11's characterisation rests on the FLIPPER's `raw_analyze` edge timings, which show genuine peaks — 63:721 / 64:532 with nothing at 78-82, and the all-ones diagnostic moving the whole peak to 79 (C383, C387). That is peak structure and a controlled contrast, not binning. ⇒ **U11's measurement is still NOT TAKEN**, and the next step is to rebuild the analysis on the project's PROVEN demodulators (`askdemod.py`, ctest's `cdemod`) instead of a hand-rolled edge detector | 1 known-good control capture, 4 void arms, 1 retracted validation | ⭐ capturing a protocol the pair provably DECODES — the only control that can catch an analyzer reporting a defect on a clean signal | L366 |
| C402 | ⛔⛔⛔ **FOUR READ ARMS ARE DOWN AND THE BOUNDARY IS NOT BIT RATE — IT IS *PROTOCOLS THIS BRANCH ADDED*. THE HOST DECODERS ALL PASS.** The new bench geometry made the decisive test possible with no hands: **#2 emulates, #1 reads**, so the fault can be reproduced on a SECOND device against a SECOND kind of signal source. Results, with an EM410X control taken before AND after: **Gallagher (ASK RF/32) 0 of 3, Securakey (ASK RF/40) 0 of 2, Noralsy (ASK RF/32) 0 of 2, GProxII (biphase RF/64) 0 of 2** — while **EM410X reads `deadbeef88`** and **Viking reads `deadbeef`**, both pre-existing upstream protocols on the SAME GPIO/comparator capture path. ⛔ **C400'S RF/32-AND-RF/40 BOUNDARY IS REFUTED**: GProxII is **RF/64**, the same rate as the passing control, and it fails. ⭐⭐ **AND IT IS NOT THE DECODER LOGIC**: `make check` passes every one of them on the host — *Gallagher ASK RF/32 decode exact*, *Securakey*, *Noralsy*, *GProxII biphase RF/64 decode exact* — against synthesized waveforms. ⇒ **The decode maths is fine and the device-side path for the branch's protocols is not.** ⭐ **Two devices, two signal sources**: C400 saw Gallagher 0/6 and Securakey 0/5 on REAL pm3-written tags read by #2, and today #1 fails the same two plus Noralsy and GProxII on emulations. The bench-geometry explanation C400 could not exclude is now dead — a different device, a different source, the same result, with a working control on each. ⚠ **NOT YET ATTRIBUTED TO A COMMIT.** The next step is the device-side scan path: EM410X and Viking reach the capture through their own upstream scan functions, while the four failing protocols go through the scan path this branch added — that difference is where to look, and a bisect is the fallback. ⚠ **BENCH TOPOLOGY CHANGED AND IT IS NOW MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE**: #2 faces #1 and **can no longer read the T5577 at all** (HID `LF tag not found` where it read FC 123 / CN 4567 an hour ago) while pm3 still reads that tag. A tick that runs a read arm on #2 against the tag will get a FALSE failure | 11 failing reads across 4 protocols, 3 control reads across 2 protocols, host suite green, 1 topology check | ⭐ reading an EMULATION from the other Chameleon — it turns a one-device anomaly into a two-device, two-source reproduction, and it only became possible because the bench moved | L367 |
| C403 | ✅ **THE FOUR DOWN READ ARMS ARE NOT MINE — PROVEN BY FLASHING PRE-SESSION FIRMWARE, NOT BY ARGUING FROM THE DIFF.** I have reflashed both devices repeatedly today, so *did the person flashing firmware all day break the read arms* is the first question a reviewer asks and it deserved a measurement. ⭐ The diff was suggestive but not sufficient: my two firmware commits touch **only** `lf_tag_em.c` (8 lines), the three FSK emitters, the new `fsk2a_mod.c/.h`, `tag_base_type.h` and the Makefile — **nothing under `rfid/reader/`**. ⚠ That is not proof, because the tag and reader paths share **PWM0**, and a base-clock change I made could in principle leak into reader mode. ⇒ **Built `940ba078`'s firmware — the last firmware commit BEFORE this session — flashed it to #1, and re-ran the arms**: **Gallagher 0 of 3 and Securakey 0 of 2, with the EM410X control reading `deadbeef88`.** Identical to HEAD. ⇒ **The defect predates this session's firmware changes**, and the PWM0 leak hypothesis is dead with it. ⭐ It also narrows attribution usefully: the fault is older than `940ba078`, so a bisect has a bounded range rather than an open one. ⚠ **What this does NOT do is exonerate the branch** — these are the branch's own protocols and its own read arms, recorded as verified (`lf securakey read` 6 of 6; C343's nineteen arms at 76 of 76). Something between those verifications and `940ba078` broke them, and it is still unattributed. ✅ Bench restored: #1 rebuilt and reflashed from HEAD, `git checkout HEAD -- firmware/` clean | 1 pre-session build + flash, 5 arms re-run with a control, 1 restore | ⭐ flashing the old build rather than reasoning from the diff — the shared-peripheral objection is exactly the kind a diff cannot answer | L368 |
| C404 | ⛔⛔⛔ **RETRACTION: C402'S BOUNDARY AND C403'S EXONERATION ARE BOTH INVALID — READING ONE CHAMELEON'S EMULATION WITH ANOTHER'S SAADC PATH CANNOT WORK BY DESIGN, AND THE FIRMWARE SAYS SO ITSELF.** Testing **Indala** — the branch's flagship read, on the same path as the four *failing* protocols — produced not a silent failure but the reader's own diagnostic: *a tag-like subcarrier is present but no frame of the requested type could be decoded ... **or an emulated tag whose subcarrier is not locked to the reader's own carrier***. ⭐ **That names the mechanism, and the hardware notes already contained it**: a real tag DIVIDES the reader's carrier so its subcarrier is inherently phase locked, while an emulating Chameleon generates PWM from its own clock — and *in reader mode the carrier is generated, not recovered; nothing in the design ever knew a reader's carrier phase*. ⇒ **The split I reported is real but I named it wrong.** It is not *branch-added vs pre-existing*; it is **needs carrier phase lock (SAADC whole-capture: indala, gallagher, securakey, noralsy, gproxii) vs does not (GPIO/comparator envelope: em410x, viking, hidprox, ioprox)** — which is why EM410X and Viking passed as *controls* that could never have failed. ⛔ **WHAT IS RETRACTED.** (1) C402's attribution, and its Noralsy and GProxII failures outright — those two were tested ONLY by emulation, so their status is **unknown, not failing**. (2) **C403's *the defect predates this session*** — it re-ran Gallagher and Securakey on pre-session firmware **through the same invalid configuration**, so it shows nothing about either build. ⚠ **I built and flashed a firmware to answer a question the method could not answer.** ✅ **WHAT SURVIVES IS C400, AND ONLY C400**: Gallagher **0 of 6** and Securakey **0 of 5** against REAL Proxmark-written TAGS read by #2, with EM410X 2/2 and HID passing on the same tag in the same position. A real tag is phase locked, so that failure is NOT explained by this and remains a genuine unattributed defect in two read arms. ⚠ **It can no longer be retested without moving #2 back to the T5577** — the rigs are mutually exclusive (C402). ⭐ **The lesson is M50's, again**: I used a new test METHOD without asking what a pass would prove, and its controls were chosen from the wrong family — EM410X could not have failed, so it validated nothing | 3 SAADC-path protocols probed, 1 firmware diagnostic read rather than grepped past, 2 claims retracted | ⭐ running INDALA — the one protocol whose failure would be unbelievable — instead of only the suspects; its message named the mechanism in one line | L369 |
| C405 | ⭐⭐ **THE BENCH IS NOW ONE LINK BY DESIGN: THE TWO CHAMELEONS FACE EACH OTHER AND NOTHING ELSE IS ON THEIR PADS.** ⚠ **Corrected by the operator, and the correction matters**: I first wrote this up as two links having *gone dead*, which reads as a fault to chase. It is not. **There is no T5577 on the Chameleons' pads and the Flipper is not in the arrangement** — the T5577 sits on the Proxmark's pad, completely separate. ⇒ `#2 cannot read the T5577` means *the tag is not there*, and the Flipper reading **0 of 3** of #1's Gallagher (6 of 6 this morning) means *the Flipper is not there*. Nothing broke. ⭐ **The measurements were still worth taking and one of them is load-bearing**: `flipper.py` ABORTS when its plugin will not load (C374) and it SCORED instead, which proves the Flipper is alive and this is geometry rather than C377's heap fault recurring — a distinction that cost a whole unit to learn. ⛔ **WHAT THE TOPOLOGY BLOCKS, WHICH IS THE POINT.** The **emulate column** (8 of 11 at 6/6) needs the Flipper as reader and cannot be re-measured; **C400's real-tag read arms** need #2 and the tag on the same pad. §1's *rig A is Flipper + Chameleon #1 ... and it WORKS, emulate needs NO bench change* is now false and is corrected. ✅ **WHAT WORKS**: `./fskcap.sh` (#1 emulates, #2 captures raw), either Chameleon reading the other's emulation **on the GPIO family only** (M52), and pm3 with the T5577 alone. ⚠ **The configurations are mutually exclusive**, so the bench is a CHOICE now and every future *needs hands* must name which one it wants | 3 links probed, 1 live control establishing the Flipper is alive | ⭐ the operator's correction — I described a deliberate rearrangement as a failure, which would have sent the next tick hunting a fault that does not exist | L370 |
| C406 | ⛔⛔⛔ **THE CONTEXT CHECK REPORTED 8% ALL SESSION WHILE THE TRUE FIGURE WAS 70%, SO THE ≥40% STOP RULE WAS SILENTLY DISABLED FOR THE ENTIRE RUN.** The operator ran `./autopilot.sh status` from their own terminal and got **`⛔ context 70% — OVER THE LINE`**; every tick of mine got **`context 8% used`** from the same script. ⭐ **The cause, confirmed not guessed**: `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is **UNSET in the agent's Bash environment**, so `autopilot.sh:74` falls back to `-c`, and `-c` resolved to a REAL BUT DIFFERENT session — `pct=8 msgs=30886`. ⛔⛔ **C370's guard cannot catch this.** It rejects `msgs=0`, which means *a fresh session*; this was a populated session with 30k message tokens, so a wrong-but-plausible number passed straight through. C370 fixed the case where the check measures NOTHING and left the case where it measures SOMEBODY ELSE. ⚠ **This is the most consequential instrument failure of the session** — worse than C373 or C377, which cost a unit each. This one disabled the single safety rule the loop has, for every tick, and would have kept doing so. ⇒ **THE FIX IS NOT ANOTHER GUARD ON THE NUMBER.** `-c` is a convenience whose meaning depends on who is asking and from where; the session must be identified EXPLICITLY. Either export `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` into the agent's environment, or have `status` refuse to print a percentage at all when it is unset — **an absent number stops the loop, a wrong one does not**. ⚠ And the operator saw it first, from outside, which is the only place it was visible | 1 environment check, 1 direct comparison of the same command run two ways | ⭐ the operator pasting their own output — the two invocations disagreed by 62 points and nothing inside the session could see that | L371 |
| C407 | ✅ **C406 IS FIXED TWO WAYS, AND THE AGENT CAN IDENTIFY ITS OWN SESSION AFTER ALL.** ⭐ **The key fact**: an agent always has its session id — it is the UUID in its scratchpad path, `/private/tmp/claude-*/<repo-slug>/<UUID>/scratchpad`. Passing it to `context_check.sh` returns **pct=71** against the operator's observed **70%**, where `-c` returned 8. ⇒ **(1) NEVER FALL BACK TO `-c`.** `status` now refuses to print a percentage when `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` is unset, says how to obtain it, and says to treat the tick as OVER THE LINE until it reads a real number — because **an absent number stops the loop and a wrong one does not**. Verified both ways: unset refuses, set reports 71% OVER THE LINE. ⇒ **(2) A STALENESS GUARD, the operator's idea**, as belt to that braces: context MOVES every tick, so a reading that never moves is measuring somebody else. ⚠ **It tests UNCHANGED, not *did not increase*, and that distinction is the whole design** — a compaction makes the number DROP, so a guard demanding an increase would cry wolf on the very next tick after every compact, which is how a check gets worked around instead of fixed (this file's own repeated lesson). ⚠ And it tracks the RAW `total=`, not the rounded percent: a percent is ~10,000 tokens at this window, so two real ticks can legitimately round the same, while `total` moves on every turn. ⭐ **Break-tested across all three cases**: a frozen reading fires on the third tick, a growing session never fires, and a compact's drop never fires. ⚠ Three consecutive, not two, so a quiet tick cannot trip it | 1 session-id verification against the operator's own figure, 2 status paths tested, 3 guard scenarios break-tested | ⭐ the scratchpad path — the id was in the agent's own environment the whole time, while the check guessed | L372 |

What survives: mixed frames lose most of their long tones in BOTH chains. ⇒ Read C411 first. Original text follows. ⭐⭐⭐ **THE FSK2a EMITTER PUTS ONE TONE ON THE COIL, MEASURED DIRECTLY FOR THE FIRST TIME — RF/8 IS THERE AND RF/10 IS ABSENT.** ⭐ **Both FSK arms, through a validated instrument**: AWID gives a peak at **60-64 us** holding 4081 of 16,983 pairs in one 4 us bin, and **nothing at 80 us** (130 counts, on the falling slope from 64); HID Prox gives the same shape, 2502 at 64 us and no peak at 80. RF/8 is 64 us and RF/10 is 80 us at a 125 kHz carrier, so the long tone is simply not being emitted. ⇒ **F12/U11 is now a MEASUREMENT rather than an inference** — C387's rate curve showed the long tone vanishing as alternation rose, and this shows what is on the air at full alternation: one tone. ⛔ The CAUSE is still not established; this locates it in the EMITTER, not the receiver. ⭐⭐ **THE PASS CRITERION WAS FIXED BEFORE ANY NUMBER WAS SEEN, AND THE ANALYSER WAS MADE TO PASS A KNOWN-GOOD REFERENCE FIRST (M50).** A band counts only as a **local maximum**, never as a bin with counts in it — which is precisely how C401 went wrong, binning a decaying curve and calling two of its bins bands. The EM410X control (a 6/6 arm) returned peaks at **512 and 1024 us**, exactly RF/64 Manchester's half-bit and full-bit, which validates the parser AND fixes the time unit as microseconds without taking it on faith. ⭐ **The resolution objection is answered by the data itself, and it had to be asked**: the control sits at 512 us while the tones under test sit at 64-80, so a single peak could have meant *the instrument cannot resolve this timescale*. It can — a 4 us bin at 64 us holding 4081 counts IS a resolved feature in that band, so an RF/10 population would have formed its own peak. That internal control is what makes the negative attributable. ⛔ **`rfid raw_analyze` was NOT used** — it wedged the Flipper twice. `flipraw.py` takes `rfid raw_read` to a file and fetches it with `storage read_chunks`, binary-safe. ⚠ `raw_read` needs a FULL PATH; the bare name answers *"File is not RFID raw file"* | 2 FSK arms + 1 control, 35,759 pairs, criterion pre-registered, parser validated on a known-good reference | ⭐ writing down what would count as a peak BEFORE looking — the previous answer to this same question was retracted for exactly the lack of it | L374 |

| C411 | ⚠ **RESOLVED BY C413 — THE CAUTION WAS RIGHT, THE CULPRIT WAS THE OTHER CHAIN.** Calibrated against a real tag, the **ASK** chain is accurate (44.4% where ~45% is expected) and the **PSK** chain is the unreliable tone meter (16.7% on that same tag). So ASK-chain numbers ARE quotable and the emitter is convicted. Original text follows. ⛔⭐ **I BLAMED THE EMITTER TOO HARD, AND THE RULE THAT CAUGHT IT IS THE PROJECT'S OWN: SUSPECT THE INSTRUMENT FIRST.** ⭐ **The measurement that corrects C409**: the SAME emission read through the Flipper's **PSK** filter chain instead of its ASK chain returns **2-3x more RF/10** — the alternating-every-bit frame goes 7.9% -> **21.6%**, the real AWID frame 0.4% -> **2.3%**. Only the RECEIVER changed. ⇒ C409's *"nothing at 80 us"* was partly the ASK chain, and **no single-chain number may be quoted as the emitted ratio**. ⭐⭐ **WHAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS STILL THE FINDING**: both chains agree on direction, and the shortfall is far too large to be instrument alone. Pure frames are CORRECT in both directions — all-zeros emits 0.1% RF/10, all-ones emits **73-85%** — so the emitter can produce either tone and both chains can resolve either. Every MIXED frame collapses: the real AWID frame should be 30.4% RF/10 (165 long tones against 378 short, exactly what `ctest/roundtrip.c` already pins) and reads 2.3% on the better chain. ⭐⭐ **AND THE SHAPE IS A CLIFF, NOT C387'S RATE CURVE.** Five alternation rates, not three: run-96 pure 73%, then run-8 **6.2%**, run-4 4.1%, run-2 5.2%, run-1 4.0%. It does not degrade *in proportion to how often the tone changes* — it falls off a cliff the moment the frame contains BOTH tone lengths, and is flat after that. C387 read three points as a slope; the fourth and fifth say otherwise. ⇒ The cause is something about a sequence MIXING 4-entry and 5-entry tones, not about rate — which kills the constant-bit-period hypothesis too: `recompute_frames_per_burst()` sums the actual `counter_top`s and assumes nothing. ⛔ **STILL OPEN, and the bench cannot currently settle it**: both available receivers are envelope detectors with their own duty-dependent bias, and mixing the tones IS a duty change (50% for RF/8, 40% for RF/10) — exactly what an AC-coupled front end with a ~27-sample time constant (C204) would distort. ⇒ **The one measurement that would calibrate them: a REAL pm3-written FSK2a tag (HID Prox on the T5577) on the FLIPPER's pad, read through both chains.** A genuine mixed-tone source scores the instrument instead of the instrument scoring us. That is one tag move | 4 frames x 2 receive chains + a 5-point rate sweep, 7 captures, ~150,000 pulse/duration pairs | ⭐ running the SAME signal through a second filter chain before believing the first — the finding was already committed when the second chain contradicted its magnitude | L375 |
| C412 | ✅⛔ **C400 DOES NOT REPRODUCE — 18 OF 18 — AND ITS CONTROLS WERE BOTH ON THE WRONG CAPTURE ENGINE.** ⭐ **The measurement**: on real tags written by the Proxmark's own encoder, with **pm3 asked to read back every write before our reader was scored** (M33), **Gallagher 6/6, Securakey 6/6, and Noralsy 6/6** — Noralsy being the third ASK RF/32 point C400 itself named as the next step. C400 recorded 0 of 11 across the first two. ⭐⭐ **IT WAS NEVER A CODE DEFECT, AND THE BISECT C400 PROPOSED WOULD HAVE FOUND NOTHING**: `git log` says **zero** commits have touched `firmware/application/src/rfid/reader/lf/` since L365, the entry that recorded the failure. The code under those arms is byte-identical to when they failed. ⛔⛔ **THE REAL LESSON IS THE CONTROL.** C400 claimed *neither capture engine is down*, resting on EM410X ("the GPIO path") and HID Prox ("the SAADC path"). **HID Prox is NOT the SAADC path.** Verified in source: `lf_hidprox_data.c` and `lf_em410x_data.c` both include `lf_reader_data.h` and never call `lf_drive_swept_read`, while `gallagher_read`, `securakey_read` and `noralsy_read` all live in `lf_indala_data.c` and all go through `lf_drive_swept_read`. ⇒ **Both controls were GPIO; both failing arms were SAADC.** The SAADC engine had NO passing arm in that session, so the one conclusion the controls were there to license was the one they could not support — *a control that cannot fail is not a control*. ⚠ `AUTOPILOT.md` §2 U5 is where this came from and it is also wrong (it says Gallagher reuses the GPIO path); fixed. ⭐ **`pm3written.sh` now closes the same hole it had**: it wrote the tag and scored our reader without ever asking the judge whether the write landed, so a silent T5577 failure and a blind reader printed the identical 0/N. | C413 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **F12 IS CONVICTED: A REAL TAG READS 44.4% ON THE SAME CHAIN WHERE OUR EMITTER READS 7.0%, SAME CREDENTIAL, SAME PAD.** ⭐ **The calibration C411 asked for, taken**: a Proxmark-written HID Prox H10301 (FC 123, CN 4567) on the Flipper's pad — a genuine mixed-tone FSK2a source carrying **the same credential our emulator was arming**, so the ideal tone composition is identical and the only variable is emitter versus tag. ⭐ **The real tag shows TWO CLEAN PEAKS of near-equal height** — 76 us: 20,218 and 60 us: 19,417 — which is what FSK2a is supposed to look like. Ours shows **one** peak at 60-64 and a scatter of spurious short durations (32, 36, 20 us) that the real tag does not produce at all. ⇒ **The ASK chain is exonerated and CALIBRATED**: 44.4% measured against ~45% expected. It was never suppressing the long tone, so C409's direction was right and C411's caution — though correct to raise — pointed at the wrong chain. ⛔ **It is the PSK chain that cannot be trusted as a tone meter**: 16.7% on the very tag the ASK chain reads at 44.4%. Every PSK-chain number in C411 is therefore an instrument artefact, including the 2-3x that prompted the correction. ⚠ **C409's *"absent"* is still too strong** — ours is 7.0%, not zero. ⇒ **F12 is a firmware defect in the emitter, no longer a suspicion**, and the cliff of C411 stands: pure frames correct both ways, every mixed frame collapsing. The cause remains open, now bounded to a sequence mixing 4-entry and 5-entry tones | 2 chains x 1 real tag + 1 emulation of the SAME credential, ~94,000 pulse/duration pairs | ⭐ the operator putting a real tag on the pad — one swap turned an unattributable shortfall into a calibrated 6.3x | L376 |
| C414 | ⚠ **ITS EVIDENCE STANDS; ITS HYPOTHESIS IS WITHDRAWN BY C415.** The four eliminations and the SEQ[0].CNT readings below are unaffected. But *a real tag's mark tracks its tone* was an OVER-READING of 44 hand-scanned pairs: measured across the whole capture the tag's mark is **30% duty on RF/8 and 63% on RF/10**, which no tag emits — the pulse width is receiver-shaped. ⇒ Read C415. Original text follows. ⭐⭐⭐ **THE FSK2a SEQUENCE REACHES THE PERIPHERAL INTACT — SO THE DEFECT IS THE WAVEFORM'S SHAPE, AND THE REAL TAG SHOWS WHAT IS WRONG WITH IT: OUR MARK DOES NOT TRACK THE TONE.** ⭐ **Four eliminations, all measured, none argued**: (1) **truncated playback is refuted** — `ff00...`, whose first eight bits are 1s, still emits only 6.2% RF/10, so it is not that the head of the frame is played and the rest dropped; (2) **the device loads the RIGHT LENGTH every time** — `hw lfdebug`'s `SEQ[0].CNT` reads **9216 / 9408 / 9340 / 9600** for all-zeros, alternating, the real AWID frame and all-ones, exactly 4x(2304+k) for k one-bits in each, so the long tones ARE in the buffer the peripheral is reading; (3) **DECODER load is WAVEFORM and the base clock is 1MHz**, both correct; (4) `recompute_frames_per_burst()` was already cleared, and it sums real `counter_top`s. ⚠⚠ **AND ONE NEAR-MISS WORTH RECORDING: `PWM0 COUNTERTOP` reads 1000 where ours is 16, which looks damning until the control is taken.** Indala PSK1 and Gallagher — both **6/6 working** arms on the same peripheral and the same WAVEFORM mode — read **1000 as well**. It is the init default, uninformative, and reading it as the bug would have cost a unit (M53's engine question, asked of a register instead of a path). ⭐⭐ **WHAT THE REAL TAG SHOWS (C413's capture, re-read for pulse rather than period): A REAL TAG'S HIGH TIME TRACKS ITS TONE AND OURS DOES NOT.** On the tag, a 76-79 us period carries a **~50 us** mark and a 62-65 us period carries a **~20 us** mark — roughly constant duty, which is what a subcarrier square wave is. Ours holds the mark **fixed at 4 carrier cycles** and varies only the gap, so duty is **50% for RF/8 and 40% for RF/10**. ⇒ **The DC average of our load modulation moves whenever the tone changes.** That is a mechanism which predicts exactly the cliff C411 measured: a PURE frame settles at one average and emits correctly in both directions, while ANY mixed frame never settles — and it predicts the rate-independence too, since what matters is that the average moves at all, not how often. ⛔ **NOT YET PROVEN — it is a hypothesis with a mechanism, not a measurement**, and the competing one (the tank's Q, which is analog and not ours) makes the same prediction. ⇒ **THE TEST IS ALSO THE FIX**: make the mark HALF THE TONE PERIOD, as a real tag does — mark 4 / gap 4 for RF/8, mark 5 / gap 5 for RF/10, constant 50% duty. That needs `counter_top` to divide 4 AND 5 carrier cycles, so 8 ticks (one carrier cycle) instead of 16, which **doubles the worst-case buffer to 4800 entries (38,400 bytes)**. ⚠ Pin it in `ctest/roundtrip.c` before it goes near hardware | 4 frames x SEQ[0].CNT, 2 working-arm register controls, 1 refutation, 2 pulse-width distributions | ⭐ taking the control on a register that looked like the answer — the arms that WORK report the same 1000 | L377 |
| C415 | ⛔⭐ **THE ENCODING IS NOT THE DEFECT — TWO DIFFERENT FSK2a ENCODINGS FAIL IDENTICALLY — AND C414'S HYPOTHESIS IS WITHDRAWN BEFORE IT COST A FIRMWARE BUILD.** ⭐⭐ **The fact that settles it, and it was sitting in the tree**: `protocols/ioprox.c` **still runs the OLD encoding** — one entry per tone with `counter_top` varying between `LF_FSK2a_PWM_HI_FREQ_TOP_VALUE` and `LO_FREQ_TOP_VALUE` (lines 346/352) — while `awid.c` and `hidprox.c` run the NEW shared builder with `counter_top` CONSTANT and the tone carried in the duty. **All three emulate 0 of 6.** ⇒ Two encodings that differ in exactly the variable C382/C383 changed produce the same failure, so the defect is **common to both** and is not the choice of encoding. ⭐ That retires the whole line C414 was about to open, and it retires it for free. ⛔⛔ **C414's MECHANISM IS WITHDRAWN.** It rested on *a real tag's mark tracks its tone*, read off 44 hand-scanned pairs. Measured over the whole capture the real tag's median mark is **19 us on its RF/8 periods and 50 us on its RF/10 periods — 30% and 63% duty**, a ratio of 2.63. No tag emits that; a subcarrier is a square wave. ⇒ **The measured pulse width is an artefact of the envelope detector's threshold, not the tag's modulation**, so nothing about the emitter's duty can be inferred from it, ours included. ⭐ **And the consequence runs the other way**: a real tag swings the detector's apparent duty FURTHER than we do (30%->63% against our 50%->40%) and still decodes perfectly on that same receiver. So *the DC average moves when the tone changes* cannot by itself be fatal — which is the mechanism C414 proposed, and it is now the weaker explanation rather than the leading one. ⚠ **A second assumption checked and false before it was written down**: *PSK1 varies `counter_top` per entry and works, so per-entry `counter_top` reaches the air*. `utils/psk1.c` sets `e->counter_top = LF_PSK1_SUBCARRIER_TOP`, a **constant**. The 6/6 PSK1 arm is no evidence about per-entry `counter_top` at all. ⇒ **WHERE THIS LEAVES F12**: the sequence reaches the peripheral intact (C414), pure frames of EITHER tone emit correctly, every mixed frame collapses regardless of alternation rate (C411), and the encoding is not the variable (here). What remains common to both encodings is the playback path in `lf_tag_em.c` and the analog side. ⚠ **Do not rebuild the emitter again until something distinguishes those two** — three encodings have now been written and the air has not changed | 2 encodings compared in source, 4 median-mark distributions over ~94,000 pairs, 1 source claim checked and refuted | ⭐ checking two assumptions I was about to build on, and both were false — the cheaper one was a `grep` | L378 |
| C416 | ⭐⭐ **THE FSK2a MIXED-FRAME FAILURE IS DETERMINISTIC, NOT MARGINAL — WHICH RETIRES THE SNR EXPLANATION AND LEAVES A LOGIC DEFECT.** ⭐ **Five repeats of the identical mixed frame**: 0.6%, 0.3%, 0.1%, 0.5%, 0.6% RF/10 — every one under 1%, a spread of half a percentage point. **Three repeats of a pure frame** as the stability control: 73.6%, 74.7%, 71.9%, CV **0.02**. ⇒ A signal marginal enough to lose its long tones would sometimes half-succeed; five of five do not. The emitter destroys tone diversity **reproducibly**. ⚠⚠ **MY OWN PRE-REGISTERED CRITERION WAS THE WRONG STATISTIC AND I AM NOT QUOTING IT.** I said *CV above 0.4 means marginal*; the mixed arm returns CV **0.47** and that number is meaningless here, because CV divides by a mean of 0.4 — near-zero means inflate it without any real scatter. ⇒ The absolute spread is what carries the result, and pre-registering a ratio against a quantity that might be ~0 is a mistake to avoid rather than a reading to report. ⭐ **What this closes**: C387/C388's depth elimination was taken with instruments later invalidated (C401), so *is it just weak?* was live again. It is not — not as an explanation for the cliff. ⇒ **WHERE F12 NOW STANDS, as a box rather than a guess**: the sequence reaches the peripheral intact and at the right length (C414); every encoding fails alike, so it is not the encoding (C415); pure frames of EITHER tone emit correctly and every mixed frame collapses regardless of rate (C411); and the collapse is deterministic (here). ⛔ **What cannot be settled with the instruments on this bench**: the Flipper's raw reader returns EDGES, not amplitude, so *the long tones are absent* and *the long tones are present but too shallow to cross the detector's threshold* look identical to it. ⇒ **The one instrument that returns amplitude is our own SAADC path** (`rdrcap.py` + `DATA_CMD_LF_READER_CAPTURE`), and it needs the two Chameleons facing. ⚠ **C408 said that arrangement served no open unit and it was right AT THE TIME** — it was wanted then for a DECODE that the phase-locked path cannot do from an emulation (M52). That commit reached `origin` having never been through a compiler. ⚠ **It does compile** — verified here, `337,012` bytes text, links clean — so the entry is a PROCESS defect, not a broken build, which is exactly the kind that survives because nothing goes red. ⇒ **The gate now compiles `firmware/` whenever the staged diff touches it**, and notes-only commits — the common case — stay instant, which is what stops it being worked around. ⚠⚠ **AND A SECOND TRAP, PAID FOR IN THE SAME MINUTE**: `firmware/build.sh` opens with `rm -rf objects`, so a build that dies on a missing tool leaves **no flashable DFU package at all**. It died twice — `GNU_INSTALL_ROOT` points at `/usr/bin/` where this host has the toolchain in `/opt/homebrew/bin`, and then `nrfutil` was not on PATH, which is **C200's exact trap a second time**. ⇒ Both the incantation and the `rm -rf` are now in `TOOLS.md`'s trap table. ⚠ The final `mergehex` step still fails and that is harmless: it is the SWD convenience artifact, written AFTER the DFU zips, so the gate checks for the artifact rather than the exit code. ⚠ **The device is still BEHIND** — `status` says so (C358) and it is right; the rebuilt `ultra-dfu-app.zip` now contains F13 but nothing has been flashed | 1 process defect, 1 firmware build verified, 2 build traps recorded | ⭐ reading what `gate` actually does instead of what its name and its ✓ implied — prompted by needing it to produce a DFU package | L381 |

That is the detail that turns C200's four misdirected flashes into two clean ones. ⚠ **A first attempt flashed a `-dirty` build** and `status` caught it — *built from a DIRTY tree, so it matches no commit* — because the package had been built while the notes edits were still uncommitted. | C420 | ⭐⭐⭐ **F12 IS NOT A CLIFF AT "ANY MIXING" — THE MINORITY TONE IS LOST AND THE MAJORITY TONE SURVIVES, AND THE CROSSOVER IS WHERE THE MAJORITY FLIPS.** ⭐ **Six AWID frames, each scored against an expectation COMPUTED FROM ITS OWN BITS** (`flipraw.py --raw --frac`), ASK chain only — the chain C413 calibrated: 100% ones → expected 100.0%, measured **73.1%**; 92% ones → 90.2% / **48.4%**; 67% ones → 62.5% / **36.2%**; 50% ones (run-8) → 45.5% / **3.3%**; 8% ones at the HEAD → 7.0% / **0.0%**; 8% ones at the TAIL → 7.0% / **0.0%**. ⇒ Long-tone survival runs **73% → 54% → 58% → 7% → 0%**: roughly flat while the long tone is the majority, then a **cliff between 67% and 50% long-tone content** — precisely where the dominant tone changes hands. ⛔ **C411's characterisation is CORRECTED**: *it falls off a cliff the moment the frame contains BOTH tone lengths, and is flat after that* is wrong in both halves. A frame of 88 ones and 8 zeros contains both lengths and delivers **48.4%**, and the region is not flat but graded. ⭐⭐ **AND C411's RATE-INDEPENDENCE IS EXPLAINED RATHER THAN MERELY CONFIRMED**: run-8/4/2/1 all sit at 4-6% because they are all **50/50 frames** — the variable was never the alternation rate, it is the COMPOSITION, and those four points hold composition constant while varying only rate. ⭐⭐ **POSITION IS NOT THE VARIABLE EITHER, TESTED AT BOTH EXTREMES**: eight one-bits at the head and eight at the tail both return 0.0% — 0 long tones of 12,404 in-band durations, and 1 of 5,445. Not suppressed, **absent**. ⭐ **THIS ALSO RESOLVES A CONFUSION BETWEEN TWO EARLIER ENTRIES.** C414 refuted truncated playback with a frame it wrote as `ff00...` reading 6.2%, which is the exact number C411 reports for **run-8** — and a true head-`ff` frame reads **0.0%**, not 6.2%, so C414's frame was `ff00ff00...`, the run-8 frame. ✅ **C414's conclusion SURVIVES and is strengthened**: a frame whose first eight bits are all ones emitting ZERO long tones refutes head-only playback harder than 6.2% did. ⚠ **A smaller correction, and it does not change any conclusion**: C411's *the real AWID frame should be 30.4% RF/10 (165 long against 378 short)* is not reproducible from the frame it names — `011d81711dd1181111111111` carries **31** one-bits, so 155 long against 390 short, **28.4%**. ⛔ **AND THAT SECOND ACCUSATION IS WITHDRAWN (C421's tick).** I wrote *its citation — exactly what `ctest/roundtrip.c` already pins — is unsupported; that file pins no such number*, on the strength of a grep for the literals `165` and `378`. They are **computed at runtime**, and `trial_fsk_duty()` prints exactly *543 tones, gaps 378 x4 + 165 x6* — C411's citation was correct. The real mismatch is narrower and duller: ctest's AWID test frame carries **33** one-bits and the frame `flipraw.py` arms carries **31**, so 30.4% is right for ctest's frame and 28.4% for ours. ⚠ Grepping for a number that a program computes is not a check that the program does not pin it. Measured 2.3% against 28.4% is still a collapse, so C411's finding stands at its stated weight. ⭐⭐⭐ **WHAT THIS DOES TO C415'S FORK — THE PLAYBACK PATH IS EXONERATED AND THE ANALOG SIDE IS CONVICTED.** C415 said *do not rebuild the emitter again until something distinguishes the playback path from the analog side*. A digital defect in playback cannot produce a survival rate that depends on frame **composition**, because the sequence buffer is structurally identical for every composition and C414 verified `SEQ[0].CNT` is correct for all of them. A graded, composition-dependent, majority-takes-all loss is an **amplitude/threshold** phenomenon, not a logic one. ⇒ That is the distinction C415 demanded, and the bar it set is now met. ⭐⭐ **AND IT REVIVES C414'S WITHDRAWN MECHANISM WITH THE SIGN MADE EXPLICIT.** `fsk2a_mod.c` holds the mark FIXED at `LF_FSK2A_MARK_CYCLES` = 4 carrier cycles for both tones, so our RF/8 is **50% duty** and our RF/10 is **40%** — by construction, visible in the source, not inferred from a capture. The weaker-duty tone is exactly the one that dies when it is in the minority, and it survives when it is the majority. ⚠ C415 withdrew this on the grounds that a real tag swings apparent duty FURTHER (30%→63%) and still decodes — but C415 itself established that the real tag's apparent duty is **receiver-shaped**, so it is not evidence about what the tag emits. A real subcarrier is a square wave at both tones; ours is not. ⛔ Still a mechanism with evidence, not a proof — the decisive test is the fix itself | 6 frames × computed expectation, ~88,000 pulse/duration pairs, 1 instrument control (EM410X 512/1024 peaks), 1 replication of C411's pure-ones 73% | ⭐ computing each frame's expectation FROM ITS BITS instead of recalling it — that is what exposed both the C414/C411 frame confusion and C411's unreproducible 30.4% | L383 |

C226 changed it to the fixed 4-cycle mark and it was **still 0 of 6**, and stated the conclusion in terms: *duty is not the cause either — the fourth hypothesis to fall.* C380 then found the fixed-mark correction had reached AWID only, fixed `hidprox.c` and `ioprox.c`, flashed it, and got **0 of 6 again with Gallagher 6/6 bracketing** — *the mark shape is NOT the cause of the FSK silence*. ⇒ Both duties have been on the air; both are silent. ⚠ **The one gap in the refutation, stated rather than hidden**: those trials ran under the OLD per-entry-`counter_top` encoding, so 50% duty under the CURRENT shared builder is not literally what was tested. But C415 established that the two encodings **fail identically**, so encoding is not the variable — and a variable that fails under one encoding is not rescued by the other. ⛔ Rebuilding the emitter for duty would be the FOURTH encoding written against unchanged air, which is precisely what C415 forbade. ⭐⭐ **AND C226 IS A STRONGER MEASUREMENT THAN THE ONE THAT DISPLACED IT.** C415 withdrew *a real tag's mark tracks its tone* because an envelope detector's apparent pulse width is receiver-shaped — correct, and it applies to C415's own 19us/50us figures too. C226 did not measure through an envelope detector: it captured a **working** AWID emission (the Flipper emitting, Chameleon #1's `lf sniff`, **decoded byte-exact by our own reader**, 1,668 periods) and found the HIGH run fixed at 4 samples on 1,207 of them while only the LOW run varied, 4 for RF/8 and 6 for RF/10. ⇒ **A fixed mark is the shape of an emission that DEMONSTRABLY WORKS on this bench**, so it cannot be what makes ours silent. That capture is committed under `caps/awid-flipper-emit/`. ⭐⭐⭐ **WHAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS C380'S QUESTION, STILL UNANSWERED**: *does the PWM peripheral actually emit what it is asked at tone periods of 8 and 10 carrier cycles, where every working emitter on this device uses 32, 40 or 64?* Gallagher 32, Securakey 40, Noralsy 32, EM410X 64 — all work; the FSK2a family switches the modulator at **15.6 kHz against ASK RF/32's 3.9 kHz**, four times faster through the same analog path. C380 flagged it as untested and nothing between C381 and C420 has tested it. ⭐⭐ **C420'S DATA IS EVIDENCE FOR IT RATHER THAN FOR DUTY.** A band-limited analog path explains majority-takes-all directly: a sustained tone reaches steady state and is detected, a minority tone amid a different switching rate never settles. That predicts the composition dependence, the 67%→50% crossover and the rate-independence **without needing the 50/40 duty asymmetry at all** — and unlike duty, it has not already been falsified on the air. ⇒ **THE NEXT UNIT IS A RATE EXPERIMENT, NOT A DUTY REBUILD**: scale the tone periods up 4x to RF/32 and RF/40, which holds the 4:5 tone RATIO and the duty pattern fixed and changes ONLY the switching rate. ⚠ It is not a valid AWID frame and no reader will decode it — it does not need to; `flipraw.py --raw --frac` measures tone composition, which is the observable | 2 prior on-air duty trials re-read from the notes (C226, C380), 1 encoding-equivalence argument (C415), 1 forward check that C380's question is still open | ⭐ reading the citation in `fsk2a_mod.h` before overturning it — the header named C226 as the reason for the fixed mark, and C226 turned out to have already killed the replacement | L384 |
| C422 | ⭐⭐⭐⭐ **F12'S CAUSE IS ESTABLISHED AT LAST, AND IT IS THE SWITCHING RATE — C380'S QUESTION, ASKED IN MARCH OF THIS FILE AND NEVER TESTED UNTIL NOW.** ⭐ **One frame, one variable.** The run-8 frame (48 one-bits, 48 zero-bits, expected **45.5%** RF/10) emitted at two tone geometries that differ in NOTHING but time scale: **RF/8 + RF/10** (switching at 15.6 / 12.5 kHz) returns **3.3%** and no long-tone peak at all; **RF/32 + RF/40** (switching at 3.9 / 3.1 kHz) returns **36.4%** and **TWO CLEAN PEAKS, 256 us and 320 us**, both passing the local-maximum test. ⭐ **What was held constant, deliberately and exactly**: the tone RATIO (4:5), the duty pattern (4 entries with 2 marked = 50%, 5 entries with 2 marked = 40% — the identical entry pattern), the pulse counts, and the worst-case entry count (2400, so F13's bounds are untouched). Only the time scale moved, by 4x. ⇒ **The variable is the RATE.** ⭐⭐ **THE CRITERION WAS PRE-REGISTERED BEFORE THE FIRMWARE WAS EVEN EDITED**: pass at ≥ 30%, fail below 10%, and anything between reported as inconclusive. 36.4%. ⭐ **Controls, all three**: pure-ones at the new geometry reads **100.0%** against 100% expected with a clean 320 us peak — and correctly shows **NO** 256 us peak, because an all-ones frame has no short tones; the EM410X instrument control returns its 512 / 1024 us peaks before and after; and #1 was re-verified functionally after the restore (peaks back at 64 / 80 us). ⭐⭐⭐ **THE RECEIVER IS EXONERATED BY A CONTROL ALREADY IN HAND.** C413 put a real pm3-written FSK2a tag on this same Flipper pad and read **44.4%** with two clean peaks at RF/8 and RF/10. So that receiver resolves those rates perfectly well from a real tag. ⇒ **It is OUR EMITTER'S ANALOG DRIVE PATH that cannot switch at 15.6 kHz**, not the encoding, not the duty, not the buffer, not the peripheral's sequence. ⭐⭐ **AND IT RETROSPECTIVELY EXPLAINS EVERY SURVIVING OBSERVATION, WHICH IS WHAT A CAUSE SHOULD DO**: C420's majority-takes-all curve (at a rate the path cannot follow, only a SUSTAINED tone accumulates enough amplitude to cross the detector; a minority tone never settles); C411's rate-independence (alternation was never the variable — composition was); C415's two encodings failing identically (both drive at the same rate); C226 and C380's two duties failing identically (likewise); C411's pure frames succeeding in BOTH directions (a single sustained rate settles); and the fact that **every emitter that works on this device runs at 32, 40 or 64 carrier cycles** — Gallagher 32, Securakey 40, Noralsy 32, EM410X 64, all at 3.9 kHz or slower. ⛔⛔ **WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, STATED PLAINLY: IT DOES NOT GIVE US A WORKING AWID EMITTER.** Real AWID *is* RF/8 and RF/10; we cannot slow it down and still be AWID. | C423 | ⭐⭐⭐ **THE KNEE IS LOCATED: RECOVERY BEGINS BETWEEN RF/10 AND RF/12 AND IS COMPLETE BY RF/16, AND IT IS A GRADUAL ROLL-OFF RATHER THAN A HARD CORNER.** ⭐ **Four geometries, one frame, one variable** — the run-8 frame (48 one-bits, 48 zero-bits, expected **45.5%** RF/10), tone ratio held at 4:5, duty pattern held at the identical 4-entry/5-entry shape with 2 marked, worst-case entries held at 2400: **RF/8+RF/10** (15.6 / 12.5 kHz) → **3.3%**, and NO long-tone peak at all; **RF/12+RF/15** (10.4 / 8.3 kHz) → **20.2%**, both peaks present; **RF/16+RF/20** (7.8 / 6.25 kHz) → **35.4%**, both peaks present; **RF/32+RF/40** (3.9 / 3.1 kHz) → **36.4%**, both peaks present. ⇒ Monotonic recovery that **saturates by RF/16** — 35.4% against 36.4% is the same answer — with **half-recovery near RF/12**. ⭐⭐ **AND THE FAILURE CHANGES KIND, NOT MERELY DEGREE, ACROSS THE KNEE.** At RF/10 the long tone produces **no local maximum whatsoever**; at RF/12 it produces a clean peak that is merely under-counted. ⇒ *Absent* and *attenuated* are different regimes, and only the RF/8+RF/10 case — the one real AWID requires — is in the first. ⭐⭐ **WHAT THIS BUYS: F12 NOW HAS A QUANTIFIED TARGET WHERE IT PREVIOUSLY HAD NONE.** AWID needs RF/8 and RF/10, which sits roughly a factor of two beyond where recovery even begins. A gradual roll-off is the signature of a **first-order or Q-limited** response rather than a hard cutoff, which is consistent with the tank or the drive stage and inconsistent with a digital limit. ⚠ **NOT A dB MEASUREMENT, AND I AM NOT QUOTING ONE**: the observable is a COUNT fraction of duration bins, not amplitude, so *half-recovery near RF/12* must not be read as a -3dB corner. Naming a corner frequency needs the amplitude path (`rdrcap.py`), which remains the one instrument here that returns amplitude. ⚠⚠ **A SECOND, SMALLER SHORTFALL IS EXPOSED AND IS NOT RATE-RELATED**: even fully recovered the measurement saturates at **~36%** against an expected 45.5%, and it is the SAME 36% at RF/16 and RF/32 — so slowing further does not close it. That residual 9 points is unexplained; the duty asymmetry our emitter has by construction (50% short, 40% long) and band-edge counting are both candidates, and it is a separate question from F12's cause | 4 geometries x 1 frame, ~55,000 pulse/duration pairs, 2 diagnostic builds flashed and reverted, restore verified functionally by tone period | ⭐ binary search rather than a linear sweep — two flashes bracketed the knee that a six-point sweep would have cost six | L386 |
| C424 | ⭐⭐⭐ **THE ELEMENT IS NAMED FROM THE SCHEMATIC, AND THE Q IT IMPLIES REPRODUCES C423'S KNEE: THE LF ANTENNA TANK.** ⭐ **The emulation path, read off `hardware/ultra/Chameleon_nrf52_ultra_V1.0.pdf` page 2 (sheet `LF`), block *LF Ant Load***: `LF_MOD` (P1.13 on hw_v1, `hw_connect.c:124`) drives the GATE of **Q3, a WNM3013-3 N-MOSFET**, which switches **R11, 220 ohm** from `LF_OA` to ground. ⚠ **`LF_OA` is NOT the coil** — block *LF Ant Resonance* puts `P_LF_ANT_RAW` across the tank capacitors **C34 2.2nF + C10 5.6nF** (C33 is NC) and then through a SERIES rectifier **VD1, 1N4148**, so Q3 loads the tank *through the diode*. ⭐ **A sanity check that this is really the 125 kHz tank**: 7.8 nF resonating at 125 kHz needs **L = 208 uH**, an ordinary LF coil. ⭐⭐ **THE SWITCH IS ELIMINATED AND THE TANK IS NOT.** R11 x the envelope chain's C28 (10nF) is **2.2 us** against a 32 us mark — about fifteen times faster than it needs to be, so neither Q3 nor R11 can be what rolls the tone off. A RESONANT TANK, by contrast, **necessarily** band-limits: its -3dB bandwidth is fc/Q, and a subcarrier at fs needs sidebands at fc ± fs to survive, so it requires **Q ≤ fc / 2fs**. ⭐⭐⭐ **AND THAT REQUIREMENT, EVALUATED, IS C423'S CURVE**: RF/32 needs Q≤16, RF/16 needs Q≤8, RF/12 needs Q≤6, RF/10 needs Q≤5, RF/8 needs Q≤4. ⇒ **A loaded tank Q of roughly 6 to 8 predicts exactly what was measured**: full response at RF/16 and RF/32, half response at RF/12, collapse at RF/10 and RF/8. The knee was not fitted to this — C423 measured it before the schematic was opened. ⚠⚠ **WHAT IS AND IS NOT ESTABLISHED, BECAUSE THE RISK HERE IS CIRCULARITY.** The schematic establishes the **mechanism** — there is a resonant tank in the emulation path and a resonant tank must band-limit. It does **NOT** establish the **value**: Q ≈ 6-8 is *inferred from the knee*, so quoting the knee back as proof of Q would be circular. ⇒ **The falsifiable prediction is that an INDEPENDENT measurement of the loaded Q returns 6-8** — a ringdown or a -3dB sweep, neither of which has been taken. ⚠ VD1 also makes the load half-wave and adds its own recovery behaviour, and this does not separate that from the tank. ⛔⛔ **THE CONSEQUENCE, IF IT CONFIRMS, IS THAT F12 IS A HARDWARE LIMIT AND NOT A FIRMWARE DEFECT.** Every encoding, duty and buffer hypothesis failed because none of them was ever the variable. Lowering the loaded Q means damping the tank — a board change, not a commit. ⭐ The firmware-side option that remains is **pre-emphasis**: the load switch is binary, but its TIMING is ours, so shaping transitions to fight the ring is possible in principle and entirely unexplored. ⭐ And it is not a universal bound — real AWID tags exist and the Flipper emits AWID well enough for our own reader to decode byte-exact (C226); this tank was presumably tuned for READING, where the fastest thing it must pass is EM410X's 1.95 kHz | 1 schematic sheet, 1 pin map, 1 resonance check, 1 switch-speed elimination, 5 Q requirements against C423's 4 measured points | ⭐ checking whether the load switch could be the limiter BEFORE blaming the tank — 2.2 us against 32 us retired it in one line | L387 |
| C425 | ⭐⭐⭐ **C424 SURVIVES THE OBJECTION THAT SHOULD HAVE KILLED IT, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT READ AND EMULATE SEE DIFFERENT LOADED Q ON THE SAME TANK.** ⛔ **The objection, raised deliberately against my own finding**: our READER reads real AWID, Paradox and Pyramid tags **byte-exact** (C201 — AWID 5/5, Paradox 4/4, Pyramid 4/4 on real T5577s), and those tags emit exactly the RF/8 and RF/10 subcarriers C424 says the tank cannot pass. If tank bandwidth were the limit it should limit READING too, and C424 would be refuted by a result already in these notes. ⭐⭐ **THE RESOLUTION IS STRUCTURAL AND COMES FROM THE REPO'S OWN SOURCES, NOT FROM ANALOG INTUITION.** `lf_125khz_radio.c:41` states it outright: *the PWM drives `LF_ANT_DRIVER`, which is the SELECT pin of the antenna's analog switch, so this value is the mark/space of the square wave that **swings the coil terminal between GND and 3V3***. And `rfid_main.c:57` **parks `LF_ANT_DRIVER` LOW for tag emulation**. ⇒ **READER mode drives the tank from a low-impedance source switching at 125 kHz; TAG mode parks the coil terminal at one rail and leaves the tank free-running.** A forced resonator's amplitude is re-established by its drive every cycle, so its loaded Q for amplitude perturbation is LOW and its modulation bandwidth WIDE; an undriven one sits at its natural Q. ⭐ Same L, same C34+C10, same Q3/R11 — **different loaded Q, and the sign is exactly the one C424 needs**: wide enough to READ a real tag's RF/8 subcarrier, too narrow to EMIT one. ⭐⭐ **AND IT DISPOSES OF A SECOND OBJECTION IN PASSING**: *a commercial AWID tag is also a passive tank and it manages RF/8*. It does — which means the claim is not that passive tanks cannot do this, but that **OURS has too high a Q**, plausible for an antenna tuned for read range and sensitivity rather than for modulation bandwidth. C226's Flipper emission is the same point from the other side. ⚠⚠ **WHAT THIS IS AND IS NOT.** It is a STRUCTURAL asymmetry, established from a schematic and two firmware sources. It is **not** a measurement: neither loaded Q has been measured, and the switch's Ron sits in series with the coil in BOTH modes, so the difference is the DRIVE and not the switch itself. ⇒ C424's falsifiable prediction is unchanged and now doubles: **tag-mode Q ≈ 6-8, reader-mode Q materially lower.** ⭐⭐ **AND IT HANDS THE NEXT UNIT A ONE-DEVICE METHOD, WHICH IS WHY THE OBJECTION WAS WORTH TAKING.** Reader-mode Q is measurable on a SINGLE Chameleon with tooling that already exists: `lf_gap.c` cuts the field on command and `rdrcap.py` returns AMPLITUDE samples from our own SAADC, so the tank's ringdown after a field cut gives Q = pi x f x tau directly. ⭐ It tests half the prediction with no bench move: **reader-mode Q must come out LOW (< ~5); if it comes out high, the read path should not work either and C424/C425 are both in trouble** | 1 objection raised against my own finding, 2 firmware sources, 1 schematic sheet, 1 one-device method identified from existing tools | ⭐ looking for the result in these notes that should REFUTE the new finding, before the next tick found it instead | L388 |
| C426 | ⛔⭐ **THE TANK'S Q IS NOT MEASURABLE WITH THE INSTRUMENTS ON THIS BOARD, AND THE CONTROL PROVES IT RATHER THAN THE FIT FAILING TO CONVERGE.** ⭐ **The method was free and needed no firmware**: `lf_reader_capture_probe()` already takes a `settle_ms` — field-ON time *before* the capture window opens — and `rdrcap.py` already defaults it to **0**, so an ordinary capture already contains the field-start transient. It is a clean monotonic decay: 8560, 7180, 5496, 4544, 4108, 3500, 2840 ... to a floor by sample 17, then a slow rise to a steady 5793. ⭐ **Least-squares fit over the decay, at one carrier-locked sample per 8 us: tau = 30.5 us.** ⭐⭐⭐ **THE CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES THIS A RESULT INSTEAD OF A NUMBER.** The same capture on **#2, sitting on the Proxmark's pad**, against **#1 facing the Flipper** — two radically different antenna loadings — gives **29.3 us against 30.5 us, the same within 4%**. ⇒ A TANK-dominated transient must change when the coupling changes, because coupling is exactly what damps a tank. This does not move. **The transient is the FILTER CHAIN, not the tank**, and its magnitude agrees: the schematic's second filter stage carries a 3k/10nF pair, which is **30 us**. ⭐⭐ **THE PRE-REGISTERED CRITERION SAID THIS WOULD BE INCONCLUSIVE AND IT IS BEING HONOURED.** I wrote before capturing that tau above ~25 us means filter-dominated and **INCONCLUSIVE, not a refutation**. It came out 30 us. The only thing extractable is a one-sided bound — the chain can only ADD delay, so tau_tank ≤ 30 us and **reader-mode Q ≤ 12** — and that bound does not discriminate: C425 predicted *materially under 5*, and ≤12 admits both that and its opposite. ⛔⛔ **SO THE REAL FINDING IS A NEGATIVE ABOUT THE BENCH, AND IT CLOSES AN OTHERWISE UNBOUNDED CHASE.** Every ADC path this device has sits BEHIND that filter chain — the envelope detector into the two op-amp stages, and `LF_RSSI` behind R12 470k and VD2, which is slower still. The chain's dominant pole (~30 us) is LARGER than the tank's whole predicted time constant (13-18 us for Q = 6-8), so the quantity is masked by construction and no amount of capture cleverness recovers it. ⇒ **Measuring this needs a scope on a test point** — the board brings out `LF_OA_OUT`, `LF_ANT_DRV`, `LF_RSSI`, `LF_MOD` and `LF_AMP_PWR` — which is external instrumentation an autopilot run does not have. ⚠⚠ **WHAT THIS DOES TO C424/C425: THEIR STANDING CHANGES AND SHOULD BE SAID PLAINLY.** They remain a well-motivated, internally consistent explanation that predicts C423's measured knee, resolves why reading works where emulating does not, and names a specific element. But their central prediction is **untested, and untestable on this bench**. ⇒ They are **CONSISTENT-BUT-UNCONFIRMED**, not established — and recording that is better than leaving an open action nothing here can close. ⭐ **WHAT REMAINS TESTABLE WITHOUT ANY OF IT**: pre-emphasis. The load switch is binary but its TIMING is ours, and shaping transitions to fight a ring is measurable by exactly the tone-composition method that produced C420 through C423 — no Q value required | 2 captures on 2 devices with different antenna loading, 14-point log-linear fits, 1 schematic cross-check, 1 pre-registered inconclusive criterion honoured | ⭐ taking the SECOND device as a coupling control — without it, 30.5 us would have been reported as the tank | L389 |
| C427 | ⛔⛔⭐ **BOTH REMAINING FIRMWARE ROUTES ARE ELIMINATED ON THE HARDWARE'S OWN TERMS, SO — IF THE TANK EXPLANATION HOLDS — F12 HAS NO FIRMWARE FIX AT ALL.** Two criteria were fixed before looking, and the schematic answers both. ⛔ **1. PRE-EMPHASIS IS UNAVAILABLE, AND THE REASON IS HEADROOM, NOT CLEVERNESS.** Pre-emphasis works by driving a signal *beyond* its steady-state value at a transition, so the attenuated fast components are boosted back. That requires headroom above the steady state. The LF section contains **exactly one modulation leg — R11 220R with Q3** (every other resistor there is RSSI, bias or filter: R12/R13 470k, R14 82, R15 75, R16 3k, R17 4.7k), and Q3 is a **binary** switch. The deepest load available is already applied whenever the load is applied. ⇒ **There is no *harder*.** ⚠ The one nuance, and it runs the wrong way: PWM-ing Q3 faster than the carrier could synthesise *shallower* effective loads, which buys relative overshoot only by surrendering absolute depth — and depth was closed as an explanation long ago (C387/C388, re-closed by C416). You cannot pre-emphasise a signal that is already at full scale. ⛔ **2. THE SWITCH IS NOT A DAMPING KNOB EITHER, WHICH WAS THE BETTER IDEA AND IT FAILS ON THE PART.** If the tank's Q is the problem, the obvious firmware attack is to damp the tank during emulation using `LF_ANT_DRIVER`, which C425 showed is the antenna analog switch's SELECT. But the part is an **SPDT with no high-Z state**, and `lf_125khz_radio.c:41` says what the throws are: it *swings the coil terminal between GND and 3V3*. **Both throws are supply rails, both AC ground, both through the same Ron.** Parking it LOW (`rfid_main.c:57`) or HIGH presents the tank the same impedance. ⇒ There is no position that damps and no position that releases, so there is no knob. ⚠ Leaving the PWM RUNNING during emulation is not damping but **transmitting** — it would inject our own carrier against the reader's, which is a different failure, not a fix. ⭐⭐ **THE CONCLUSION IS CONDITIONAL AND THE CONDITION IS LOAD-BEARING.** *If* C424/C425's tank explanation holds, no firmware change fixes F12 — not the encoding (C415), not the duty (C226/C380/C421), not the buffer (F13), not pre-emphasis, not switch damping. That is a complete enumeration of the firmware surface. ⚠ And C426 established the tank explanation is **unconfirmed and unconfirmable on this bench**. ⇒ **F12's honest status: cause EXPLAINED but not PROVEN; every firmware route eliminated; no further firmware work justified.** ⭐ What would reopen it: a scope on `LF_OA_OUT` or `LF_ANT_DRV` returning a loaded Q far from 6-8, which would put the explanation back in doubt and the firmware surface back on the table | 2 criteria fixed in advance, 1 schematic section enumerated component by component, 1 part-type constraint, 1 firmware source | ⭐ asking what pre-emphasis physically REQUIRES before trying to build one — the answer was headroom, and a binary switch has none | L390 |

C422 answered that question by holding ratio, duty and buffer constant and moving only the tone RATE. Worst band error **3.8 points**, and the model accounts for **99.1%** of every period captured in all three. ⛔⛔ **C242'S MECHANISM IS KILLED BY THE MOSTLY-ZEROS FRAME SPECIFICALLY.** C242 held that *a biphase 0 is a HELD level and this PWM emits nothing for one*. A 92-zero-bit frame is 92 consecutive held levels — the worst case its mechanism describes — and it emitted **89.4% in the 1024us band**, exactly as predicted. | C434 | ⚠⛔ **PAC'S BIT-LEVEL DECODE IS INCONCLUSIVE AND THE DECODER IS THE UNVALIDATED PART — NO FIRMWARE DEFECT IS BEING CLAIMED.** ⭐ **What IS established, from source and free**: PAC's T5577 config is `T5577_MODULATION_DIRECT | T5577_BITRATE_RF_32` with **no inversion bit**, so unlike FDX-B (C430) PAC is NOT an inverted protocol and that fix does not transfer. And `pac_build_bitstream` is fully specified: **8 sync ones, then 12 UART frames of start(0) + 7 data LSB-first + odd parity + stop(1)** = 8 + 120 = 128 bits, over a payload of STX, '2', '0', 8 ASCII card bytes and an XOR of the card bytes. ⭐ **The attempt**: decode the emitted capture's NRZ runs at 256us/bit, align on the END of each run of >=8 ones (the sync is NINE ones on air, because the previous frame's stop bit abuts it — aligning on the run's START finds the sync and then fails, which is how the first attempt went wrong), and score 128 bits against the expectation. **Best: 97 of 128 inverted, 70 of 128 as-captured, first divergence at bit 10 in both.** ⛔⛔ **WHY THAT IS NOT A RESULT.** The decoder has **no control**: nothing here shows it can recover a bitstream KNOWN to be correct, so a mismatch cannot yet be attributed to the emitter rather than to the decode. Two concrete reasons to distrust it before the firmware: (1) the Flipper biases individual RUNS — high long by ~96us and low short by the same — and C429/C430 established that only PERIODS are unbiased, yet this decode necessarily works in single runs; (2) the recovered pattern looks **stretched**, long zero runs where the expectation alternates, which is the signature of a bit-period or alternation error in the decoder rather than of wrong payload bits. ⭐⭐ **THE CONTROL THAT WOULD SETTLE IT, AND IT IS THE C429 METHOD**: emit a SECOND PAC credential and check that the recovered bits CHANGE exactly as the two expectations differ. That validates the decoder differentially without needing it to be absolutely right, and no single-credential capture can substitute. ✅ Capture saved at `/tmp/pac_cap.raw` (1023 pairs, 6 resyncs) so the next attempt costs no bench time. ⇒ **PAC's status is unchanged from C433**: it EMITS, with correct NRZ run structure at multiples of the 256us bit; whether the bits it carries are right is still open | 1 T5577 config read, 1 bitstream spec read end to end, 1 alignment bug found and fixed mid-unit, 2 polarities scored, 1 decoder left unvalidated on purpose | ⭐ refusing to convert 97/128 into a firmware defect when the instrument's known run-length bias and an unvalidated decoder both sit between the emitter and that number | L397 |

PASS was: recovered difference set R equals predicted D **exactly**. ⭐ The control was chosen to be capable of failing: `0000AAAA` vs `CARD0001` gives **|D| = 38 over bits 39..126 with total ones near-equal (52 vs 54)**, so the difference is in POSITION and no level-counting decoder passes by luck; `CARD0002` was rejected in design at 4 bits. ⛔ **Result, and it still stands: FAIL both polarities.** As-captured |R|=49 with **12 violations inside the invariant prefix**; inverted |R|=19 against |D|=38 (9 extra, 28 missing). ⇒ The decoder does not track the data, so C434's 97/128 means nothing. ⛔⛔ **WHAT IS RETRACTED**: this entry claimed the cause was that *the Flipper reports edge positions and not levels*, on the strength of periods quantising 99.6% against runs at 0.3-2.6%. **That comparison was not like-for-like** — the periods were bias-corrected and the runs were not.
