This entry only fixes **membership**: six FSK2a emulate arms are silent, and two of them were sitting outside the list that explains why. ✅ Consequence for the emulate column: C431's 12-pass / 4-silent result is **fully attributed** once PAC resolves — every other silent arm is FSK2a and retired. | 2 emitter sources classified, 1 clock-membership macro read, 1 re-grade result reconciled arm by arm | ⭐ reconciling C431's SILENT list against the retirement list instead of assuming the two matched — two arms had been unexplained for sixteen findings | L410 |
| C448 | ⭐⭐ **THE PAC EMULATE ARM'S *SILENT* VERDICT WAS PRODUCED BY A CRITERION THAT COULD NOT HAVE PASSED — AND THE FLIPPER'S OWN DECODER RULES OUT THE LONG-DC CEILING FROM SOURCE.** ⛔ **The harness defect first, because it is C177/M28 in a third place.** `flipgrade.py`'s pac arm armed `--cn CARD0001` and required the token `CARD0001` in the decode. Momentum's renderer is `furi_string_printf(result, "CIN: %08lX", bit_lib_get_bits_32(protocol->data, 0, 32))` and `protocol->data` is **four bytes** produced by `hex_chars_to_uint8(asciiCardId, ...)` — **the Flipper never prints the eight ASCII characters, only their hex VALUE**, and `CARD0001` contains an `R`, which is not a hex digit, so it cannot even round-trip. ⇒ a perfectly working PAC emulation would have graded **WRONG-VALUE**, and *a criterion that cannot pass is not a criterion*, exactly as a control that cannot fail is not a control (M52, C441). ✅ **Fixed and re-measured**: credential `1337BEEF` (eight uppercase hex), token `1337BEEF`, which is exactly what `CIN:` carries. **NULL silent, fdxb PASS `ID: 999-000000001337`, pac SILENT** — so the SILENT verdict reproduces under a criterion that could now have passed, and the harness defect did **not** cause it. ⭐⭐ **AND THE SECOND HALF OF C443'S DISJUNCTION DIES IN THE FLIPPER'S SOURCE, WITH NO BENCH MOVE.** `protocol_pac_stanley_decoder_feed` accepts durations from `PAC_STANLEY_MIN_TIME` **60us** to `PAC_STANLEY_MAX_TIME` **4000us**, quantises at `PAC_STANLEY_CYCLE_LENGTH` **256us**, and has an explicit pair of branches for a run of **>= 9 bit periods** — clamping the first to 8 to align the buffer and passing the next through at 9 so bit 127 carries the stop bit and bits 128-135 the next preamble, which `can_be_decoded` requires. ⇒ **the decoder is BUILT for PAC's 2304us static sync run**; 2048us and 2304us are comfortably inside its window. ⛔ So *the Flipper cannot represent long DC* is refuted as an explanation of PAC's silence, and C443's queued bench unit can no longer be read as a test of that. ⚠ This is the decoder, not the analogue front end — what the comparator does with a long static level is a separate question and is measured in C449. | 1 Momentum protocol source read end to end (decoder window, >=9 branch pair, renderer, data width), 1 harness criterion replaced, 1 re-grade with null + a same-geometry control | ⭐ reading the READER's source before re-running the arm — the token had been unmatchable through C431, C433-C443 and the standing prompt's whole open-work list | L412 |
| C449 | ⭐⭐⭐ **PAC'S EMISSION IS NOT A DEGRADED FRAME — IT IS TOO LONG AND TOO HIGH, AND C439'S LIVE HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED IN ITS STATED DIRECTION.** ⭐ **Two numbers that need no alignment and no decode, each predicted from the emitter's own modulator before the capture** (new `airduty.py`): **MAX RUN** — the longest single level on the air, against the longest the frame can contain; **DUTY** — the fraction of TIME the air is high, against the frame's fraction of 1-bits. Every earlier PAC instrument (pacdiff's differential, C441's tiling, C440's alignment) presupposed the emission was the frame degraded; these ask whether it is the frame at all. ⛔⛔ **THE CONTROLS ARE WHAT MAKE THE NUMBERS READABLE, AND BOTH LAND ON PREDICTION.** `fdxb.c` — PAC's exact geometry, counter_top 32, one entry per bit, whole-entry held level via `counter_top + 1`, proven byte-exact (C430) — predicted max static **384us**, measured **364us**. `gproxii.c` predicted **512us**, measured **661us**. ⭐ **The comparator bias is estimated FROM those controls rather than guessed**: their true duty is fixed at **50%** by construction (Manchester and biphase spend half of every bit at each level), and they imply **b = 43us and 89us**, consistent with C435's fitted 93us and 151us. ⛔⛔⛔ **PAC, through the identical chain in the same session: `pac.c`'s own frame for `1337BEEF` is 128 bits with 58 ones ⇒ predicted max run 2304us and predicted high-time 45.3%. Measured max HIGH run 6021us — OVER BY 3717us, 2.6x the longest run the frame can contain — and high-time 85.7%.** The bias that would reconcile it is **883us**, ten to twenty times the controls' own range and 3.5 bit periods; at b = 200us, double the largest control, it is still 76.6%. ⇒ **no comparator bias explains it.** ⭐⭐ **WHAT THIS RELOCATES.** C439's live hypothesis was that a long static stretch costs an AC-coupled path its baseline and **swallows** the runs after it — that predicts observed runs SHORTER than intended. The measurement is the **opposite sign**: the emission is dominated by long HIGH runs the frame does not contain (340 of 1023 pulses >= 2000us, p95 5954us), with short ~155us lows between them. ⇒ the deficit is **run-LENGTHENING / high-merging, not run-losing**, and C439's hypothesis is refuted as stated. ⭐ It also explains the SILENT verdict mechanically: C448 measured the Flipper's PAC decoder rejecting any duration over **4000us** before pushing a bit, and **p95 of our PAC high runs is 5954us** — most of the emission is discarded at the door. ⛔⛔ **NO MECHANISM IS ASSERTED AND THE SIDE IS NOT ESTABLISHED.** An envelope detector that holds high through a long static stretch and merges the short runs after it produces exactly this signature, so **emitter and receive chain are both still live** — three mechanism-shaped claims in this investigation (C435, C438, C440) did not survive their own rechecks. ⇒ **C443's bench unit is still wanted but its READING is rewritten**: the discriminator is no longer *real tag at full run count while the emulation is short* — the emulation is not short — but **max run and duty against the frame's own 2304us and 45.3%**, measured through this same Flipper chain. | 3 arms x 4s captures through one chain in one session, 2 controls scored against source predictions, 1 bias estimated from the controls rather than assumed, 1 predicted duty computed live from the emitter's own bitstream builder | ⭐ estimating the comparator bias from the controls instead of quoting C435's fitted number — the whole verdict is *883us is not a bias*, and that sentence is empty without a measured range to compare it to | L412 |

  ASK/Manchester — Gallagher **10/10**, Securakey **3/3**, Noralsy **3/3** → **16 of 16**
  PSK2, differential — Indala224 **14 of 16**
  PSK1, absolute — Indala26 **2/8**, NexWatch **1/10**, Keri **0/3** → **3 of 21**

⛔ **C189 called this an ASK-versus-PSK split on n=2. It is not.** Indala224 is PSK and reads as well as the ASK protocols; what separates it is that it is DIFFERENTIAL. ⭐⭐ **The mechanism is the project's own, written in `psk1.h` long before this measurement**: "the differential needs no polarity search: XOR of consecutive bits is invariant under global inversion, which is the whole point of differential encoding." A free-running emitter's phase drift relative to the reader's carrier IS a slowly-varying global rotation — differencing removes it, amplitude never saw it, and absolute phase is exactly what it destroys (C74). ✓ **The Proxmark reads REAL PSK1 T5577 tags perfectly** — Indala26, Keri and NexWatch all night off the tag — so its demodulator is sound and the fault is our emitter's clock, not its reader. ⚠ **One confound, stated rather than buried: Indala224 is also the longest frame** (224 bits against 64-96), and this bench has no short PSK2 protocol to separate frame length from differential encoding. The mechanism argument is strong; the experiment does not isolate it | 61 Proxmark reads across 7 protocols and 3 encodings | ✓ the ASK protocols are the same-device same-session control at 16/16, and real PSK1 tags read by the same Proxmark are the control for its demodulator | ✓ the Flipper reads every one of these emulations 6/6 to 10/10, so the disagreement is between READERS and localises the fault to a clock the Flipper tolerates and the Proxmark does not | L153 |
| The `lf hid prox read` 0/15 episode | ⛔ **unexplained, and not reproducible.** It sat at 0/15 for ~15 minutes, survived 150 s of rest, then cleared on its own. C25 excludes the antenna, the field, coupling and thermal drift; the control arm excludes `lf indala read`. What remains is the hidprox decoder or something above it. `lfprobe.py` will catch it in the act if it recurs |
| `lf hid prox read` lock rate | ⭐⭐ **CLOSED — it was the BLE advertising burst, and `cf745fb` fixed it.** 96/96 exact with the guard, 71/80 without it on the same tag in the same session (C250). ⛔ What replaces it as an open question is worse: with the guard off, 5 of those 9 came back as WRONG CREDENTIALS, past a parity check that was working (C251) |
| Does `lf indala read` false-positive on a **non-Indala tag**? | HID Prox closed (C24). ⚠ EM410x, ioProx, Viking, PAC and Jablotron are all still untested, and ASK/OOK tags modulate the envelope differently from HID's FSK |
| Is the phase window tag- or unit-specific? | ⚠ worse than that — it is **not stable across sessions on the same tag and unit**. Phase 12 was 5/5 in the sweep and 4/10 correct a day later; phase 28 went the other way. ⇒ do not hard-code a phase, rotate. Still untested on a second tag or unit |
| Indala parity | reported, deliberately **not** a gate — see C19. It only covers format 26, and a badly wrong frame passed it |

## What runs on the device

| | |
|---|---|
| `rfid/reader/lf/lf_indala_psk.c` | the demodulator. Pure integer, no nRF dependency, so it host-compiles for `research/indala-psk-read/ctest/` |
| `rfid/reader/lf/lf_indala_data.c` | capture, phase rotation, the two-capture agreement rule |
| `rfid/reader/lf/lf_reader_generic.c` | `capture_begin`/`capture_end` extracted so `lf sniff` and the Indala read share one copy of the BLE suspend, the ring and the settle discard |
| `DATA_CMD_INDALA_SCAN` = 3033 | `lf indala read` |

Cost: 8 KB of `.bss` for the sample buffer, ~1 KB of stack, 92 KB of RAM still free.
The decode is ~32 x 4096 adds and runs inside the inter-capture gap.

⭐ **The filter is free.** Summing a [1,2,1]-filtered signal over a 32-sample window is
identically a weighted sum of the *unfiltered* signal over 34 samples with weights
`1,3,4,4,...,4,3,1`, so the notch folds into the bit integrator as

```
integrator = 4*sum(y[a..a+31]) + y[a-1] - y[a] - y[a+31] + y[a+32]
```

— four extra adds per bit rather than three per sample, and no second buffer.

## Hardware reference

⛔⛔ **EVERY PATH FROM THE LF ANTENNA TO THE MCU PASSES THROUGH A DETECTOR DIODE. NO PIN CARRIES
A CARRIER-RATE SIGNAL, IN ANY MODE.** Stated first because it forecloses a whole family of
designs — carrier-edge field detection, carrier locking, coherent demodulation — and because
this project proposed one of them anyway, against a reference section that already said
`VD1 detector` (C134, M32). Source: `Chameleon_nrf52_ultra_V1.0.pdf`, sheet 2.

```
                       U1 RS2257XC6, SPDT analog switch
P_LF_ANT_DRV -- A            B1 -- GND        select S <- P0.31  LF_ANT_DRIVER  [OUTPUT]
                             B2 -- VCC3.3V

P_LF_ANT_RAW --+-- C33 / C34 / C10 resonance --|>|-- LF_OA
                                               VD1  1N4148, half-wave
LF_OA --+-- R11 220 -- Q3 drain, gate <- P1.13  LF_MOD  [OUTPUT]   load modulation
        +-- R12 470k --------------------- LF_RSSI -> AIN0 / LPCOMP0   P0.02
        +-- R13 470k -- GND
        \-- C28 10n / R9 82 / C35 27n / C36 33n -> IC1A (R17 4k7 / C38 1n)
                                                -> IC1B -> LF_OA_OUT -> AIN5   P0.29
                                                   both powered from LF_AMP_PWR = P1.15
```

**All four LF pins, and what each one can and cannot see:**

| pin | dir | what it is |
|---|---|---|
| P0.31 `LF_ANT_DRIVER` | **out** | the SELECT input of the analog switch. The coil's drive terminal is tied to GND or 3V3 on the *far side* of U1 — this pin never touches antenna signal, so it cannot be re-purposed as a sense input |
| P1.13 `LF_MOD` | **out** | gate of Q3, which shunts `LF_OA` through 220 Ω — the RECTIFIED node, not the coil |
| P0.02 `LF_RSSI` | in | `LF_OA` through 470 k, clamped by VD2. Post-detector: an envelope |
| P0.29 `LF_OA_OUT` | in | the same envelope, twice filtered. Dead unless P1.15 is high, and tag mode clears it (`rfid_main.c`) |

- ⭐ **VD1 is the whole reason.** It rectifies at the coil, and LF_OA's own load sets the hold:
  R12 ∥ R13 = 235 kΩ against C28 in series with C35 ≈ 7.3 nF → **τ ≈ 1.7 ms**, which is the
  "~2 ms peak detector" `lf_tag_em.c` already waits out between bursts. The number was in the
  firmware before the schematic was read; they agree.
- ⚠ **Doubly blocked.** Even if carrier ripple survived the detector, the SAADC is clamped to
  200 kHz (`lf_125khz_radio_saadc_rate_set`) and a 125 kHz carrier needs 250 kHz.
- ⭐ **Modulation and sensing share ONE node.** Q3 pulls `LF_OA` down; LF_RSSI and the op-amp
  chain both read `LF_OA`. That is not an implementation accident to work around — it is why
  LPCOMP must be disabled while emulating.
- In reader mode the carrier is **generated, not recovered**: TIMER2 counts our own
  `PWMPERIODEND` events. Nothing in the design ever knew a reader's carrier phase.
- Filter poles: R9/C36 = **58.8 kHz**, R17/C38 = **33.9 kHz**. At fc/2 = 62.5 kHz they cost
  −3.3 dB and −6.4 dB.
- `READER_POWER` (P1.15) is the schematic's `LF_AMP_PWR`; it feeds the bias divider
  R8 4k7 / R10 3k → LF_VBIAS ≈ 1.2 V.
- SAADC: 14-bit, PPI-triggered from the carrier PWM `PWMPERIODEND`, `GAIN1_6` against the
  internal 0.6 V reference → 3.6 V full scale, `ACQTIME_5US`.
- AIN5 sits at ~1.2 V (33% FS). AIN0 sits at **3.51 V (97% FS)** — 1/6 is already the
  lowest gain the SAADC offers, so single-ended there is no setting with more headroom.

## The tag

`a0000000e6bd0e92` — Indala, 64-bit, Fmt 26, FC 52, Card 63612. PSK1, RF/32, subcarrier at
fc/2 = 62.5 kHz. T5577 block 0 = `00081040`.

⛔ **The campaign registry's `PSK1` cell writes `DEADBEEF/12345678`** — correct PSK1 air
shaping, but not an Indala frame. Use the `INDALA26` cell for anything demod-related;
misreading this invalidated a day (L31).

Confirm before trusting any measurement:

```bash
cd /Users/Shared/code/personal/rfid/proxmark3 && ./pm3 -c "lf indala reader"
```
